<a id='question-051'></a>

### Question 051: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-01 (LYMPHADENOPATHY)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between various types of lymphoma based on morphology, immunophenotype, genetic features, and clinical presentation, specifically focusing on the characteristic features of Burkitt lymphoma and its association with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) and MYC translocation.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass on his jaw, accompanied by fever, night sweats, and weight loss over the past 3 weeks. He reports no significant past medical history and denies any known exposures or family history of malignancy. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender, rapidly growing mass involving the mandible, along with generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1800 U/L, normal <250 U/L), hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL), and anemia (Hgb 9 g/dL, normal 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are interspersed with numerous tangible body macrophages (TBMs) and have a high mitotic rate. Immunohistochemistry shows the tumor cells positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, but negative for BCL2. Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus on chromosome 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following molecular alterations is most characteristic of this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32) resulting in overexpression of CCND1
(B) t(14;18)(q32;q21) resulting in overexpression of BCL2
(C) t(8;14)(q24;q32) resulting in overexpression of MYC
(D) t(15;17)(q24;q21) resulting in overexpression of PML-RARA
(E) t(9;22)(q34;q11) resulting in overexpression of BCR-ABL1

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** Young adult male from Equatorial Africa. This demographic is epidemiologically linked to endemic Burkitt lymphoma, often associated with EBV.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), generalized lymphadenopathy. This constellation suggests an aggressive lymphoma. The jaw involvement is characteristic of endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH, hyperuricemia, anemia. These are common findings in high-grade lymphomas due to rapid cell turnover and tumor lysis. Hyperuricemia is particularly prominent in Burkitt lymphoma due to massive cell death.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm ("starry sky" appearance due to TBMs), high mitotic rate. This morphology is classic for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+. This immunophenotype is characteristic of mature B-cell lymphomas, specifically Burkitt lymphoma. BCL2 negativity helps distinguish it from follicular lymphoma.
- **Genetic Analysis:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) detected by FISH. This translocation juxtaposes the MYC oncogene (on chromosome 8) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (IGH on chromosome 14), leading to MYC overexpression. This is the hallmark genetic abnormality of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most characteristic molecular alteration. Based on the clinical, histological, immunophenotypic, and genetic findings, the patient has Burkitt lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. Its pathogenesis is driven by the dysregulation of the MYC proto-oncogene.
- **MYC Translocation:** The characteristic genetic abnormality is a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8q24. The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), which places MYC under the control of the strong immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) enhancer, leading to constitutive and high-level MYC overexpression. Less common variants include t(2;8)(p12;q24) involving the kappa light chain locus (IGK) and t(8;22)(q24;q11) involving the lambda light chain locus (IGL).
- **MYC Function:** MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. Its overexpression leads to uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibits differentiation, contributing to the aggressive nature of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **EBV Association:** Endemic Burkitt lymphoma, common in equatorial Africa, is strongly associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection. EBV infection is thought to contribute to the development of Burkitt lymphoma by promoting B-cell proliferation and potentially facilitating the MYC translocation.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The rapid growth, high mitotic rate, high LDH, and hyperuricemia are direct consequences of the uncontrolled proliferation and high cell turnover driven by MYC overexpression.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): t(11;14)(q13;q32) resulting in overexpression of CCND1. This translocation is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma (MCL). It places the cyclin D1 (CCND1) gene under the control of the IGH enhancer, leading to cyclin D1 overexpression, which drives cell cycle progression. MCL typically presents in older adults and has a different immunophenotype (CD5+, CD10-, Cyclin D1+).
- (B): t(14;18)(q32;q21) resulting in overexpression of BCL2. This translocation is the hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma (FL). It juxtaposes the BCL2 anti-apoptotic gene with the IGH locus, leading to BCL2 overexpression, which inhibits apoptosis and promotes cell survival. FL typically presents with indolent, widespread lymphadenopathy and has a characteristic immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+).
- (D): t(15;17)(q24;q21) resulting in overexpression of PML-RARA. This translocation is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). It creates the PML-RARA fusion protein, which disrupts myeloid differentiation. APL presents with pancytopenia, coagulopathy (DIC), and Auer rods in promyelocytes.
- (E): t(9;22)(q34;q11) resulting in overexpression of BCR-ABL1. This translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome, is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and a subset of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). It creates the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene, encoding a constitutively active tyrosine kinase that drives uncontrolled myeloid proliferation. CML typically presents with leukocytosis, splenomegaly, and progresses through chronic, accelerated, and blast phases.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Option C) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (Option A) | Follicular Lymphoma (Option B) | APL (Option D) | CML (Option E) |
|----------------------|-----------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|----------------|----------------|
| **Characteristic Translocation** | t(8;14)(q24;q32)          | t(11;14)(q13;q32)              | t(14;18)(q32;q21)              | t(15;17)(q24;q21)| t(9;22)(q34;q11)|
| **Gene Involved**    | MYC                         | CCND1 (Cyclin D1)              | BCL2                           | PML, RARA      | BCR, ABL1      |
| **Resulting Protein**| MYC Overexpression          | Cyclin D1 Overexpression       | BCL2 Overexpression             | PML-RARA Fusion| BCR-ABL1 Fusion|
| **Cell Type**        | Mature B-cell               | Mature B-cell                  | Mature B-cell                  | Myeloid Progenitor| Myeloid Progenitor|
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults       | Older Adults                   | Middle-Aged/Older Adults        | Variable      | Middle-Aged/Older Adults|
| **Clinical Features**| Rapidly growing mass (jaw), B symptoms, high LDH, hyperuricemia | Lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly, B symptoms | Indolent lymphadenopathy, widespread | Pancytopenia, DIC | Leukocytosis, splenomegaly |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2- | CD5+, CD10-, Cyclin D1+        | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+              | CD34+, MPO+, Auer rods | CD34+, Myeloperoxidase+ |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by a MYC translocation (most commonly t(8;14)) leading to MYC overexpression, resulting in aggressive proliferation. Key features include rapid growth, jaw involvement (endemic form), "starry sky" histology, high LDH/uricemia, and a specific immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-). Differentiating it from other lymphomas requires knowledge of their characteristic translocations and resulting molecular consequences (e.g., t(11;14) in MCL, t(14;18) in FL, t(15;17) in APL, t(9;22) in CML).

### Question 052: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-02 (Lymphadenopathy is lymph node enlargement due to reactive hyperplasia, infiltration, or destruction)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically the role of the MYC oncogene translocation and its downstream effects on cell proliferation and apoptosis. (理解伯基特淋巴瘤的分子发病机制，特别是MYC癌基因易位的作用及其对细胞增殖和凋亡的下游影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass on his jaw, accompanied by fever, night sweats, and weight loss over the past 3 weeks. He reports no significant past medical history and denies any known exposures or family history of malignancy. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender, rapidly growing mass involving the mandible, along with generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions. Laboratory studies show elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1800 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14. Which of the following molecular events is most likely responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Amplification of the BCL2 gene leading to inhibition of apoptosis.
(B) Translocation involving the RUNX1 gene, resulting in abnormal B-cell development.
(C) Translocation involving the MYC gene, leading to constitutive cell proliferation.
(D) Mutation of the TP53 gene, causing loss of cell cycle control.
(E) Fusion of the BCR and ABL1 genes, resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** Young adult male from Equatorial Africa. This geographic location is endemic for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms). Rapid growth is characteristic of high-grade lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma. Jaw involvement is common in the endemic variant.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender mass, generalized lymphadenopathy. Consistent with lymphoma infiltration.
- **Labs:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia. High LDH indicates high cell turnover and tumor burden, common in aggressive lymphomas. Hyperuricemia results from massive cell lysis.
- **Biopsy:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with characteristic "starry sky" appearance (implied by the description of cells and context, though not explicitly stated), round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm. This morphology is classic for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunophenotype:** CD20+, CD10+, CD5-, BCL2-. CD20 and CD10 are B-cell markers. CD5 negativity helps distinguish it from mantle cell lymphoma. BCL2 negativity is crucial, distinguishing it from follicular lymphoma (which typically shows t(14;18) involving BCL2).
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (IgH) on chromosome 14. This is a hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma, where the IgH locus is involved in translocations with MYC.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event responsible for pathogenesis.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The clinical presentation (rapidly growing mass, B symptoms, endemic region), morphology (medium-sized lymphoid cells, starry sky pattern), immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-), and cytogenetics (translocation involving IgH locus on chromosome 14) are all highly suggestive of Burkitt lymphoma.
- Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation involving the MYC proto-oncogene (located on chromosome 8) and one of the immunoglobulin loci (IgH on chromosome 14, IgK on chromosome 2, or IgL on chromosome 22).
- The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), which places the MYC gene under the control of the strong IgH enhancer.
- This translocation leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein.
- MYC is a transcription factor that regulates genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, metabolism, and apoptosis.
- Overexpression of MYC drives uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibits differentiation, leading to the rapid growth characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- The BCL2 protein is an anti-apoptotic protein. Its overexpression, typically due to the t(14;18) translocation seen in follicular lymphoma, inhibits apoptosis and contributes to the indolent nature of that disease. Burkitt lymphoma is typically BCL2 negative.
- RUNX1 is involved in hematopoiesis, and mutations can cause leukemia, but it's not the primary driver of Burkitt lymphoma.
- TP53 is a tumor suppressor gene involved in cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. Mutations are common in many cancers but are not the defining molecular event in Burkitt lymphoma.
- The BCR-ABL1 fusion gene results from the Philadelphia chromosome translocation t(9;22) and is characteristic of chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) and some acute lymphoblastic leukemias (ALL).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Amplification of the BCL2 gene leading to inhibition of apoptosis. This mechanism is characteristic of follicular lymphoma, which typically involves the t(14;18) translocation. The patient's immunophenotype (BCL2-) and clinical presentation (rapid growth) are inconsistent with follicular lymphoma.
- (B) Translocation involving the RUNX1 gene, resulting in abnormal B-cell development. RUNX1 mutations are associated with certain types of acute myeloid leukemia (AML) and familial thrombocytopenia, not Burkitt lymphoma.
- (D) Mutation of the TP53 gene, causing loss of cell cycle control. TP53 mutations are common in many cancers, including some lymphomas, but the defining molecular event in Burkitt lymphoma is the MYC translocation. While MYC overexpression can indirectly affect cell cycle control, the primary mechanism is constitutive proliferation driven by MYC.
- (E) Fusion of the BCR and ABL1 genes, resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. This is the hallmark of chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) and Philadelphia chromosome-positive ALL, not Burkitt lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma                                  | Follicular Lymphoma                             | CML/Ph+ ALL                                     |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| **Key Translocation** | t(8;14), t(2;8), t(8;22) (MYC involved)           | t(14;18) (BCL2 involved)                        | t(9;22) (BCR-ABL1 involved)                     |
| **Primary Gene**     | MYC (Oncogene)                                    | BCL2 (Oncogene)                                 | BCR-ABL1 (Oncogene)                              |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-                               | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+                              | CD20+/CD10+ (ALL), CD20+ (CML blast crisis)     |
| **Clinical Course**  | Rapidly progressive, aggressive                  | Indolent, slow progression                      | Chronic phase, accelerated phase, blast crisis |
| **Morphology**       | Medium cells, starry sky pattern                 | Follicular pattern, centroblasts/centrocytes    | Myeloid precursors (CML), Lymphoblasts (ALL)    |
| **LDH**              | Markedly elevated                                 | Often elevated, but less dramatically than Burkitt | Variable                                        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by MYC translocation (most commonly t(8;14)), leading to MYC overexpression and rapid cell proliferation. Differentiating it from follicular lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2 overexpression, indolent course) and CML/Ph+ ALL (t(9;22), BCR-ABL1 fusion) is crucial based on cytogenetics, immunophenotype (especially BCL2 status), and clinical presentation.

<a id='question-053'></a>

### Question 053: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-03 (neoplasia.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the role of the BCR-ABL fusion protein as a constitutively active tyrosine kinase and its inhibition by tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs). (考察慢性髓性白血病 (CML) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCR-ABL 融合蛋白作为持续活性的酪氨酸激酶的作用及其被酪氨酸激酶抑制剂 (TKIs) 抑制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety for the past 3 months. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals marked splenomegaly. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 180,000/µL with a left shift, including numerous myelocytes and metamyelocytes. Hemoglobin is 10 g/dL, and platelet count is 600,000/µL. A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) confirms the presence of the BCR-ABL fusion gene. The patient is started on imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor. Which of the following molecular targets is directly inhibited by imatinib in this patient?

(A) JAK2
(B) FLT3
(C) BCR-ABL
(D) KIT
(E) ALK

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (constitutional symptoms), and splenomegaly. These symptoms are classic for myeloproliferative neoplasms.
- **CBC Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180,000/µL) with a left shift (immature granulocytes like myelocytes and metamyelocytes present in peripheral blood). Anemia (Hgb 10 g/dL) and thrombocytosis (platelets 600,000/µL) are also present. This constellation strongly suggests CML.
- **Diagnostic Tests:**
    - **Cytogenetics:** Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)(q34;q11)) is the hallmark genetic abnormality of CML.
    - **FISH:** Confirms the BCR-ABL fusion gene, which results from the translocation.
- **Treatment:** Imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI), is initiated.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific molecular target of imatinib in this patient's condition (CML).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CML Pathogenesis:** CML is characterized by the reciprocal translocation between chromosomes 9 and 22, t(9;22)(q34;q11). This translocation creates the Philadelphia chromosome and results in the fusion of the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 with the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9.
- **BCR-ABL Fusion Protein:** The *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase protein (BCR-ABL). Unlike the normal ABL1 kinase, which requires specific activation signals, BCR-ABL is always "on," leading to uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells.
- **Imatinib Mechanism:** Imatinib (Gleevec) is a first-generation TKI specifically designed to inhibit the ATP-binding site of the BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase. By binding to this site, imatinib prevents ATP from binding and thus blocks the kinase activity of the BCR-ABL protein, halting the downstream signaling pathways that drive CML cell proliferation and survival.
- **Other Options:**
    - **JAK2:** A tyrosine kinase involved in cytokine receptor signaling. Mutations (e.g., V617F) are common in polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, and primary myelofibrosis. Inhibitors like ruxolitinib target JAK2.
    - **FLT3:** A receptor tyrosine kinase. Mutations (e.g., ITD, TKD) are found in acute myeloid leukemia (AML) and are targets for inhibitors like midostaurin or gilteritinib.
    - **KIT:** A receptor tyrosine kinase. Mutations are found in gastrointestinal stromal tumors (GISTs), mastocytosis, and some AML cases. Imatinib also inhibits KIT, but it's not the primary target in CML.
    - **ALK:** A receptor tyrosine kinase. Rearrangements (e.g., EML4-ALK) are found in non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) and anaplastic large cell lymphoma (ALCL). Inhibitors like crizotinib target ALK.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): JAK2 is a tyrosine kinase, but its mutations are characteristic of other myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPNs) like polycythemia vera and essential thrombocythemia, not CML. Imatinib does not primarily target JAK2.
- (B): FLT3 is a receptor tyrosine kinase frequently mutated in AML. While TKIs exist for FLT3 mutations, imatinib is not a primary FLT3 inhibitor, and FLT3 is not the cause of CML.
- (D): KIT is a receptor tyrosine kinase targeted by imatinib, particularly in GISTs. However, in the context of CML, the defining molecular abnormality is BCR-ABL, which is the specific target of imatinib in this disease.
- (E): ALK is a receptor tyrosine kinase involved in certain cancers like NSCLC and ALCL. It is not associated with CML, and imatinib is not the primary treatment for ALK-rearranged cancers (though it can inhibit ALK).

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature          | CML                                     | Polycythemia Vera (PV)                 | AML (FLT3-ITD)                         | GIST                                  | NSCLC (ALK+)                          |
|-------------------|------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22) / BCR-ABL1 fusion gene        | JAK2 V617F mutation                   | FLT3-ITD mutation                     | KIT mutation                          | ALK rearrangement (e.g., EML4-ALK)    |
| **Primary Molecular Target** | BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase              | JAK2 tyrosine kinase                  | FLT3 tyrosine kinase                  | KIT tyrosine kinase                   | ALK tyrosine kinase                   |
| **Typical Treatment** | Imatinib (TKI)                          | Hydroxyurea, Ruxolitinib (JAK inhibitor) | Chemotherapy, FLT3 inhibitors         | Imatinib (TKI)                        | Crizotinib, Alectinib (ALK inhibitors) |
| **Hallmark Clinical Features** | Leukocytosis, left shift, splenomegaly | Erythrocytosis, pruritus, splenomegaly | Blasts in bone marrow/blood, fatigue | GI bleeding, abdominal pain, mass     | Cough, dyspnea, lung mass             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The hallmark of CML is the BCR-ABL fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase resulting from the Philadelphia chromosome translocation. Imatinib is a specific tyrosine kinase inhibitor targeting BCR-ABL, revolutionizing CML treatment. Remember to differentiate CML from other MPNs (like PV, ET, PMF) and AML based on genetic abnormalities (BCR-ABL vs. JAK2 vs. FLT3, etc.) and their respective molecular targets.

<a id='question-054'></a>

### Question 054: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-04 (Acute NonspecificLymphadenitis)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the pathophysiology and molecular mechanisms underlying acute nonspecific lymphadenitis (NSL), specifically the role of viral infections (like EBV) in driving B-cell proliferation and the resulting histological features, differentiating it from neoplastic processes like Burkitt lymphoma. (考察急性非特异性淋巴结炎的病理生理机制，特别是病毒感染（如EBV）驱动B细胞增殖以及由此产生的组织学特征，并将其与像伯基特淋巴瘤这样的肿瘤性疾病区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 10-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, sore throat, fatigue, and generalized lymphadenopathy. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he is not on any medications. Physical examination reveals bilateral cervical lymphadenopathy (largest node measuring 3 cm), tonsillar exudates, and mild splenomegaly. Vital signs show a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F), heart rate of 110 bpm, respiratory rate of 20 breaths/min, and blood pressure of 100/60 mmHg. Laboratory tests show a white blood cell count of 18,000/µL with 60% lymphocytes, 20% atypical lymphocytes, and normal hemoglobin and platelet counts. A monospot test is positive. An excisional biopsy of a cervical lymph node is performed. Histopathology shows diffuse effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a monotonous population of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, and inconspicuous nucleoli. Scattered large, atypical lymphocytes with irregular nuclear contours and prominent nucleoli are also present. Immunohistochemistry reveals that the monotonous lymphoid cells are positive for CD20 and CD19, and negative for CD10 and BCL6. The atypical large cells are positive for CD30.

What is the most likely underlying molecular mechanism driving the lymphadenopathy in this patient?

(A) Translocation involving the MYC gene leading to constitutive activation of cell cycle regulators.
(B) Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection leading to polyclonal B-cell proliferation and activation of atypical lymphocytes.
(C) Somatic mutation in the immunoglobulin heavy chain variable region gene leading to a monoclonal B-cell population.
(D) Aberrant expression of BCL2 protein due to a t(14;18) translocation, inhibiting apoptosis.
(E) Defective DNA mismatch repair leading to microsatellite instability and accumulation of mutations.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 10-year-old boy - Age group common for infectious mononucleosis (IM).
*   **Symptoms:** Fever, sore throat, fatigue, generalized lymphadenopathy - Classic symptoms of IM.
*   **Physical Exam:** Cervical lymphadenopathy, tonsillar exudates, mild splenomegaly - Typical signs of IM.
*   **Labs:** Leukocytosis with lymphocytosis and atypical lymphocytes, positive monospot test - Strongly suggestive of IM, typically caused by EBV.
*   **Lymph Node Biopsy:** Diffuse effacement, monotonous medium-sized lymphoid cells (CD20+, CD19+), scattered large atypical lymphocytes (CD30+) - Histology consistent with acute nonspecific lymphadenitis (NSL), often seen in IM. The monotonous population represents reactive B-cells, while the large atypical cells are activated B-cells/immunoblasts responding to the infection. The CD20+/CD19+ profile confirms B-cell origin. The negativity for CD10 and BCL6 helps rule out follicular lymphoma or Burkitt lymphoma. CD30 positivity on the atypical cells is characteristic of reactive processes like IM and Hodgkin lymphoma, but in the context of the overall picture, it points towards reactive proliferation.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the underlying molecular mechanism.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Acute Nonspecific Lymphadenitis (NSL):** This is a reactive lymphoid proliferation, typically triggered by infections (most commonly EBV, but also CMV, HIV, toxoplasmosis) or autoimmune diseases.
*   **EBV Pathogenesis:** EBV primarily infects B lymphocytes. The virus encodes proteins (like LMP1, LMP2A, EBNA1-6) that mimic cellular signals, leading to B-cell activation, proliferation, and inhibition of apoptosis. This results in a polyclonal expansion of B-cells and T-cells (specifically CD8+ cytotoxic T-cells responding to infected B-cells) within the lymph nodes, causing the characteristic histological features of NSL.
*   **Mechanism in this Case:** The clinical presentation (age, symptoms, signs, positive monospot, atypical lymphocytes) and histological findings (polyclonal B-cell proliferation with reactive features) strongly point to EBV-induced infectious mononucleosis causing acute nonspecific lymphadenitis. The molecular mechanism is the direct effect of EBV infection on B-cells, leading to their proliferation and activation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Translocation involving the MYC gene leading to constitutive activation of cell cycle regulators:** This describes the mechanism of Burkitt lymphoma, typically involving t(8;14) or variants. Burkitt lymphoma presents with a monotonous population of medium-sized cells with round nuclei, but the cells are typically CD10+ and BCL6+, and the proliferation is monoclonal, not polyclonal reactive. This patient's histology (CD10-, BCL6-) and clinical context (IM symptoms) make Burkitt lymphoma unlikely.
*   **(C) Somatic mutation in the immunoglobulin heavy chain variable region gene leading to a monoclonal B-cell population:** This is characteristic of chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL) or B-cell lymphomas. While lymphomas can cause lymphadenopathy, the acute presentation, IM symptoms, polyclonal nature (implied by the mix of cell types and clinical context), and specific histological features (CD20+/CD19+ monotonous cells, CD30+ atypical cells) are inconsistent with a typical monoclonal B-cell malignancy arising from a V(D)J rearrangement.
*   **(D) Aberrant expression of BCL2 protein due to a t(14;18) translocation, inhibiting apoptosis:** This is the hallmark of follicular lymphoma. Follicular lymphoma typically presents as painless lymphadenopathy, often in older adults, and the histology shows neoplastic follicles effacing the normal architecture. The cells are usually CD10+ and BCL6+. This mechanism and clinical presentation are inconsistent with the patient's acute illness and histological findings.
*   **(E) Defective DNA mismatch repair leading to microsatellite instability and accumulation of mutations:** This mechanism is associated with Lynch syndrome and certain types of colorectal cancer and endometrial cancer. It is not directly related to the pathogenesis of acute nonspecific lymphadenitis or the common hematologic malignancies.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Acute Nonspecific Lymphadenitis (EBV) | Burkitt Lymphoma (MYC) | Follicular Lymphoma (BCL2) | CLL (IgVH mutation) |
| :---------------------- | :------------------------------------ | :--------------------- | :------------------------ | :------------------ |
| **Clinical Context**    | Acute illness (IM symptoms)            | Rapidly growing mass   | Insidious lymphadenopathy | Older adults, fatigue |
| **Cellular Origin**     | B-cells (reactive)                    | B-cells (neoplastic)   | B-cells (neoplastic)      | B-cells (neoplastic) |
| **Proliferation**       | Polyclonal                            | Monoclonal             | Monoclonal                | Monoclonal          |
| **Histology**           | Diffuse, mixed cell types, atypical cells | Diffuse, monotonous, "starry sky" | Follicular pattern       | Diffuse, small lymphocytes |
| **Key IHC Markers**     | CD20+, CD19+, CD30+ (atypical)        | CD10+, BCL6+, MYC+      | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+        | CD5+, CD19+, CD23+    |
| **Genetic Abnormality** | Viral infection (EBV)                 | t(8;14) MYC            | t(14;18) BCL2              | IgVH mutation       |
| **Mechanism**           | Viral-induced B-cell proliferation    | MYC overexpression     | BCL2 overexpression       | Apoptosis resistance |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Acute nonspecific lymphadenitis is a reactive process, most commonly caused by EBV infection (infectious mononucleosis). The underlying mechanism involves viral infection of B-cells leading to polyclonal proliferation and activation. Key differentials include neoplastic processes like Burkitt lymphoma (MYC translocation, CD10+, BCL6+), follicular lymphoma (BCL2 translocation, CD10+, BCL6+), and Hodgkin lymphoma (Reed-Sternberg cells, CD30+, CD15+). Understanding the clinical context, histology, and immunophenotype is crucial for differentiation.

<a id='question-055'></a>

### Question 055: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-05 (Acute nonspecific lymphadenitisproduces tender enl)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically the role of the MYC oncogene translocation and its downstream effects on cell proliferation and apoptosis. (理解伯基特淋巴瘤的分子发病机制，特别是MYC癌基因易位的作用及其对细胞增殖和凋亡的下游影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 10-year-old boy from a rural area of equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass on his jaw, causing facial asymmetry and difficulty chewing. He also reports intermittent fevers and night sweats over the past month. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass measuring approximately 8 cm in diameter on the right mandible. He has generalized, non-tender lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions. His spleen is palpable 3 cm below the left costal margin. Laboratory tests show elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1200 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (9 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are negative for CD20 and CD10, but strongly positive for CD45 and Ki-67 (proliferation index >90%). Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most directly responsible for the uncontrolled proliferation observed in this patient?

(A) Inhibition of the Rb tumor suppressor protein, leading to dysregulation of the G1/S cell cycle checkpoint.
(B) Activation of the BCL-2 anti-apoptotic protein, preventing caspase activation and promoting cell survival.
(C) Upregulation of the p53 tumor suppressor protein, inducing cell cycle arrest and apoptosis in damaged cells.
(D) Downregulation of the NF-κB signaling pathway, reducing the transcription of genes involved in inflammation and cell survival.
(E) Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway, leading to constitutive cytokine receptor signaling and increased cell proliferation.

#### Correct Answer: A

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 10-year-old boy from equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, facial asymmetry, difficulty chewing, intermittent fevers, night sweats, generalized lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly. These symptoms point towards a rapidly proliferating lymphoma.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia are characteristic of high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, common in Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with specific nuclear features (round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm). This morphology is classic for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunophenotype:** CD45 positive (leukocyte marker), CD20 negative (B-cell marker, atypical for Burkitt, but can occur), CD10 negative (B-cell marker, atypical for Burkitt, but can occur), Ki-67 >90% (very high proliferation index). The high Ki-67 is a hallmark.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32). This translocation places the MYC oncogene under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain enhancer, leading to MYC overexpression.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular mechanism responsible for uncontrolled proliferation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The translocation t(8;14)(q24;q32) is the defining genetic event in the majority of endemic Burkitt lymphoma cases.
- This translocation juxtaposes the MYC proto-oncogene (located at 8q24) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (located at 14q32).
- The IgH locus contains highly active enhancer elements that drive transcription of immunoglobulin genes.
- Due to the translocation, these IgH enhancers now inappropriately drive the transcription of the MYC gene, leading to its significant overexpression.
- MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of numerous genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, metabolism, and apoptosis.
- One of the key functions of MYC is to promote cell cycle progression. It achieves this, in part, by binding to the promoter region of the cyclin D gene (CCND1) and increasing its transcription.
- Cyclin D forms a complex with cyclin-dependent kinase 4 (CDK4) or CDK6. This complex phosphorylates the retinoblastoma protein (Rb).
- Rb is a tumor suppressor protein that normally binds to and inhibits the E2F transcription factor family. E2F is required for the transcription of genes necessary for the G1/S phase transition of the cell cycle.
- When Rb is phosphorylated by the Cyclin D-CDK4/6 complex, it releases E2F.
- Free E2F then activates the transcription of genes required for S-phase entry, driving the cell from G1 into S phase.
- Overexpression of MYC leads to increased Cyclin D levels, resulting in hyperphosphorylation and inactivation of Rb.
- Inactivation of Rb allows E2F to drive uncontrolled cell cycle progression through the G1/S checkpoint, leading to rapid and uncontrolled cell proliferation, characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Inhibition of the Rb tumor suppressor protein, leading to dysregulation of the G1/S cell cycle checkpoint.** This is the correct mechanism. MYC overexpression leads to increased Cyclin D, which phosphorylates and inactivates Rb, releasing E2F and promoting G1/S transition.
- (B): **Activation of the BCL-2 anti-apoptotic protein, preventing caspase activation and promoting cell survival.** While BCL-2 is an anti-apoptotic protein often overexpressed in lymphomas (e.g., follicular lymphoma due to t(14;18)), it is not the primary mechanism driving the *proliferation* in Burkitt lymphoma caused by MYC translocation. MYC can influence apoptosis, but the dominant effect leading to rapid proliferation is Rb inactivation.
- (C): **Upregulation of the p53 tumor suppressor protein, inducing cell cycle arrest and apoptosis in damaged cells.** p53 is a tumor suppressor. Its upregulation would *inhibit* proliferation and promote apoptosis, which is the opposite of what occurs in Burkitt lymphoma. MYC can sometimes antagonize p53 function, but p53 upregulation is not the mechanism of proliferation.
- (D): **Downregulation of the NF-κB signaling pathway, reducing the transcription of genes involved in inflammation and cell survival.** NF-κB is typically involved in promoting cell survival and proliferation. Downregulation would be expected to inhibit tumor growth. NF-κB is often constitutively activated in other lymphomas (e.g., diffuse large B-cell lymphoma), but its downregulation is not the mechanism in Burkitt lymphoma.
- (E): **Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway, leading to constitutive cytokine receptor signaling and increased cell proliferation.** The JAK-STAT pathway is activated by cytokines and growth factors and promotes cell proliferation and survival. While it can be involved in some hematologic malignancies (e.g., myeloproliferative neoplasms, some T-cell lymphomas), it is not the primary pathway dysregulated by the MYC translocation in Burkitt lymphoma. The core mechanism is MYC-driven cell cycle dysregulation via Rb.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (MYC translocation) | Follicular Lymphoma (BCL-2 translocation) | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) | Myeloproliferative Neoplasms (JAK2 mutation) |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|
| **Primary Driver**   | MYC overexpression (t(8;14))          | BCL-2 overexpression (t(14;18))           | Variable (often NF-κB activation)      | JAK2 mutation (e.g., V617F)                 |
| **Mechanism**        | Rb inactivation -> G1/S dysregulation | Apoptosis inhibition                    | Variable, often cell cycle dysregulation | Constitutive cytokine signaling             |
| **Cell Cycle**       | Uncontrolled proliferation (Ki-67 high) | Slower proliferation (Ki-67 lower)        | Variable                               | Variable                                    |
| **Apoptosis**        | Can be affected, but proliferation is key | Inhibition is key                       | Variable                               | Variable                                    |
| **Key Pathway**      | MYC-Rb-E2F                            | BCL-2-Bax-Caspase                        | NF-κB                                 | JAK-STAT                                    |
| **Typical Morphology**| "Starry sky" pattern                  | Follicular pattern                       | Diffuse sheets of large cells          | Variable, often hypercellular bone marrow   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(8;14) translocation in Burkitt lymphoma leads to MYC overexpression, which drives uncontrolled cell proliferation by inhibiting the Rb tumor suppressor protein and dysregulating the G1/S cell cycle checkpoint. Remember to differentiate this mechanism from other lymphoma drivers like BCL-2 (t(14;18) in follicular lymphoma) which primarily inhibits apoptosis, or NF-κB activation (common in DLBCL) which promotes survival and proliferation through different pathways.

<a id='question-056'></a>

### Question 056: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-06 (focal involvement is seen with bacterial lymphaden)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between various types of lymphomas based on histological features, immunophenotype, and genetic translocations, specifically focusing on the characteristic features of Burkitt lymphoma versus other aggressive lymphomas like diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, fever, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss (10 lbs). He reports feeling generally unwell and fatigued. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any significant family history of malignancy. Physical examination reveals a large, palpable mass in the lower abdomen, extending to the midline. The mass is firm and non-tender. Vital signs show a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F) and a heart rate of 110 bpm. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 850 U/L (normal range: 120-250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (uric acid 9.5 mg/dL, normal range: 3.5-7.0 mg/dL). A CT scan of the abdomen and pelvis confirms a large retroperitoneal mass encasing the aorta and inferior vena cava. Biopsy of the abdominal mass reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages, creating a "starry sky" appearance. Immunohistochemical staining shows the tumor cells positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, and negative for BCL2. Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus on chromosome 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following molecular alterations is most characteristic of this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32) resulting in overexpression of CCND1
(B) t(14;18)(q32;q21) resulting in overexpression of BCL2
(C) t(8;14)(q24;q32) resulting in overexpression of MYC
(D) t(15;17)(q24;q21) resulting in overexpression of PML-RARA
(E) t(9;22)(q34;q11) resulting in overexpression of BCR-ABL1

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** Young adult male (28 years old) presenting with rapid onset symptoms.
*   **Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal mass, fever, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), fatigue. These suggest an aggressive malignancy, likely lymphoma.
*   **Physical Exam:** Large, palpable abdominal mass.
*   **Labs:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia are characteristic of high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, often seen in aggressive lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Imaging:** Large retroperitoneal mass encasing major vessels, indicating aggressive local invasion.
*   **Histology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, "starry sky" appearance (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic tumor cells). This morphology is highly suggestive of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell lineage), CD10+ (germinal center origin), BCL6+ (germinal center origin), MUM1+ (plasma cell differentiation marker, often positive in Burkitt), BCL2- (important negative marker distinguishing Burkitt from follicular lymphoma).
*   **FISH:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) confirms the characteristic translocation of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the most characteristic molecular alteration.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   The clinical presentation (rapidly growing mass, B symptoms, high LDH, hyperuricemia), histological features ("starry sky" pattern), immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2-), and FISH result (t(8;14)) are all classic for Burkitt lymphoma.
*   Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma.
*   The hallmark genetic abnormality is a translocation involving the *MYC* gene on chromosome 8 (specifically 8q24) and one of the immunoglobulin loci, most commonly the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus on chromosome 14 (14q32). This translocation, t(8;14), leads to the juxtaposition of the *MYC* oncogene under the control of strong immunoglobulin gene enhancers.
*   This results in constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein, a transcription factor that regulates cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism. Overexpression of MYC drives the rapid proliferation characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
*   The question asks for the molecular alteration characteristic of this condition. Based on the FISH result, t(8;14) leading to MYC overexpression is the correct answer.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) t(11;14)(q13;q32) resulting in overexpression of CCND1: This translocation is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. It involves the *CCND1* gene (encoding Cyclin D1) on chromosome 11 and the IGH locus on chromosome 14. Mantle cell lymphoma typically presents in older adults and has a different immunophenotype (CD5+, CD10-, Cyclin D1+).
- (B) t(14;18)(q32;q21) resulting in overexpression of BCL2: This translocation is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma. It involves the *BCL2* gene on chromosome 18 and the IGH locus on chromosome 14. Overexpression of BCL2, an anti-apoptotic protein, inhibits programmed cell death and contributes to the indolent nature of follicular lymphoma. The immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+) and histology (follicular pattern) are distinct from Burkitt lymphoma. The patient's BCL2- staining rules this out.
- (D) t(15;17)(q24;q21) resulting in overexpression of PML-RARA: This translocation is characteristic of Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). It involves the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 and the *RARA* gene (encoding the retinoic acid receptor alpha) on chromosome 17. The fusion protein PML-RARA disrupts myeloid differentiation. This is a myeloid malignancy, not a B-cell lymphoma.
- (E) t(9;22)(q34;q11) resulting in overexpression of BCR-ABL1: This translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome, is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and is also found in some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL) and occasionally Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). It involves the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 and the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9, creating the *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene, which encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. This is a myeloid or lymphoid malignancy, but the clinical presentation, histology, immunophenotype, and specific translocation (t(8;14)) in the vignette point away from this diagnosis.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Correct) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma | Follicular Lymphoma | APL (AML M3) | CML/ALL (Ph+) |
| :------------------ | :------------------------- | :------------------- | :------------------ | :----------- | :------------ |
| **Translocation**   | t(8;14)                    | t(11;14)             | t(14;18)            | t(15;17)     | t(9;22)       |
| **Gene Involved**   | *MYC*                      | *CCND1*              | *BCL2*              | *PML, RARA*  | *BCR, ABL1*   |
| **Overexpressed Protein** | MYC                       | Cyclin D1            | BCL2                | PML-RARA     | BCR-ABL1      |
| **Cell Lineage**    | B-cell                     | B-cell               | B-cell              | Myeloid      | Myeloid/Lymphoid |
| **Histology**       | "Starry sky"               | Mantle zone pattern  | Follicular pattern  | Auer rods    | Variable     |
| **Immunophenotype** | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2- | CD5+, CD10-, Cyclin D1+ | CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+  | CD34+, MPO+  | Variable     |
| **Clinical Course** | Very aggressive            | Indolent/Aggressive  | Indolent            | Acute        | Chronic/Acute |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by the t(8;14) translocation leading to MYC overexpression, resulting in rapid proliferation and a "starry sky" histology. Differentiating it from other aggressive lymphomas like DLBCL (often lacks specific translocations or has MYC/BCL2/BCL6 rearrangements) and indolent lymphomas like follicular lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2+) requires careful histological and immunophenotypic analysis, along with cytogenetic/FISH studies. Remember the key features: young age, rapid growth, B symptoms, high LDH, starry sky, CD10+, BCL2-, t(8;14).

<a id='question-057'></a>

### Question 057: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-07 (be neutrophilswithin the lymph node.Cat scratch fe)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathophysiology and histological features of Cat Scratch Disease (CSD), specifically the characteristic granulomatous inflammation with stellate microabscesses caused by *Bartonella henselae*, and differentiating it from other causes of lymphadenopathy.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old female veterinarian presents to the clinic with a 3-week history of localized, tender swelling in her left axilla. She reports a minor scratch on her left forearm approximately 4 weeks prior to the onset of symptoms, sustained while treating a stray kitten. She denies fever, chills, night sweats, or weight loss. Her past medical history is unremarkable, and she takes no medications. Physical examination reveals a firm, tender lymph node measuring 2.5 cm in diameter in the left axilla. The overlying skin is erythematous but intact. Vital signs are normal. Fine needle aspiration (FNA) of the lymph node shows numerous neutrophils, histiocytes, and occasional lymphocytes, forming small, star-shaped collections (microabscesses) within a background of reactive lymphoid tissue. Gram stain of the aspirate is negative for bacteria. A Warthin-Starry silver stain is performed on a subsequent biopsy specimen.

Which of the following histological findings is most characteristic of the underlying pathology?

(A) Reed-Sternberg cells within a mixed inflammatory background.
(B) Large, atypical lymphocytes with prominent nucleoli and irregular nuclear contours.
(C) Granulomas with central necrosis surrounded by epithelioid histiocytes and Langhans giant cells.
(D) Stellate microabscesses composed of neutrophils surrounded by palisading histiocytes.
(E) Diffuse sheets of small, mature lymphocytes infiltrating the lymph node parenchyma.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 28-year-old female veterinarian. Occupation increases risk of exposure to zoonotic diseases.
*   **Presenting Complaint:** 3-week history of tender left axillary lymphadenopathy.
*   **History:** Minor scratch on left forearm 4 weeks prior, sustained while treating a stray kitten. This is a classic exposure history for Cat Scratch Disease (CSD).
*   **Symptoms:** Localized, tender swelling. Absence of systemic symptoms (fever, chills, night sweats, weight loss) is typical for uncomplicated CSD.
*   **Physical Exam:** Firm, tender 2.5 cm left axillary lymph node with overlying erythema. Consistent with regional lymphadenitis.
*   **FNA Findings:** Numerous neutrophils, histiocytes, and lymphocytes forming small, star-shaped collections (microabscesses). This description strongly suggests stellate microabscesses. Gram stain negative rules out common bacterial infections.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most characteristic* histological finding.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Cat Scratch Disease (CSD):** Caused by *Bartonella henselae*, a gram-negative bacterium transmitted primarily through scratches or bites from infected cats (especially kittens).
*   **Pathogenesis:** The bacteria are phagocytosed by macrophages and dendritic cells at the inoculation site. These cells migrate to regional lymph nodes, carrying the bacteria.
*   **Histopathology:** The hallmark histological feature in affected lymph nodes is the formation of granulomas. These granulomas are typically poorly formed and contain central areas of necrosis surrounded by neutrophils, forming characteristic **stellate microabscesses**. The microabscesses are surrounded by palisading histiocytes and reactive lymphoid cells. Warthin-Starry silver stain is often positive for *B. henselae*, although it can be negative.
*   **Clinical Correlation:** The patient's history (veterinarian, cat scratch), clinical presentation (regional lymphadenopathy), and FNA findings (neutrophils, histiocytes, microabscesses) are all highly suggestive of CSD. The question asks for the characteristic histological finding, which is the stellate microabscess.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Reed-Sternberg cells within a mixed inflammatory background:** This is characteristic of Hodgkin lymphoma. While Hodgkin lymphoma can present with lymphadenopathy, the patient's history (cat scratch) and FNA findings (microabscesses) are not typical for Hodgkin lymphoma. Reed-Sternberg cells are large, binucleated cells with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli ("owl's eye" appearance).
*   **(B) Large, atypical lymphocytes with prominent nucleoli and irregular nuclear contours:** This description is suggestive of high-grade non-Hodgkin lymphoma, such as diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). Again, the clinical history and specific FNA findings (microabscesses) point away from lymphoma and towards CSD.
*   **(C) Granulomas with central necrosis surrounded by epithelioid histiocytes and Langhans giant cells:** This describes caseating granulomas, characteristic of tuberculosis (TB) or certain fungal infections. While CSD involves granulomas, they are typically *non-caseating* and feature stellate microabscesses, not central necrosis surrounded by epithelioid histiocytes and Langhans giant cells.
*   **(E) Diffuse sheets of small, mature lymphocytes infiltrating the lymph node parenchyma:** This pattern is characteristic of chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL) or small lymphocytic lymphoma (SLL). This is inconsistent with the acute presentation, history of a cat scratch, and FNA findings showing neutrophils and microabscesses.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Cat Scratch Disease (CSD)                                  | Hodgkin Lymphoma (HL)                               | Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma (NHL)                            | Tuberculosis (TB)                                     | Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Lymphoma (CLL/SLL) |
| :---------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------ |
| **Etiology**            | *Bartonella henselae* (cat scratch)                        | EBV, genetic factors                                 | Variable (genetic, viral, environmental)               | *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*                           | Malignant clonal proliferation of B-lymphocytes    |
| **Histology**           | Stellate microabscesses, granulomas (non-caseating)        | Reed-Sternberg cells, mixed inflammatory infiltrate | Variable (diffuse sheets, follicular pattern, etc.)    | Caseating granulomas, epithelioid histiocytes, Langhans giant cells | Diffuse infiltration by small, mature lymphocytes |
| **Key Cells**           | Neutrophils, histiocytes, lymphocytes                     | Reed-Sternberg cells                                 | Atypical lymphocytes                                   | Epithelioid histiocytes, Langhans giant cells, lymphocytes | Small, mature lymphocytes                           |
| **Clinical Presentation** | Regional lymphadenopathy after cat scratch                | Lymphadenopathy, B symptoms (fever, night sweats, weight loss) | Lymphadenopathy, B symptoms, extranodal involvement | Pulmonary symptoms, lymphadenopathy, systemic symptoms | Lymphadenopathy, fatigue, often asymptomatic       |
| **Stain**               | Warthin-Starry (may be positive for *B. henselae*)         | CD30+, CD15+ (RS cells)                               | Variable (CD20+, CD3+, etc.)                          | Acid-fast bacilli (AFB) stain                          | CD5+, CD19+, CD23+                                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Cat Scratch Disease is a common cause of regional lymphadenopathy following a cat scratch, characterized histologically by stellate microabscesses. Differentiating CSD from other causes of lymphadenopathy like lymphoma (HL/NHL), tuberculosis, or other infections requires careful consideration of the clinical history and specific histological features. Remember the classic triad: cat exposure, regional lymphadenopathy, and characteristic stellate microabscesses.

<a id='question-058'></a>

### Question 058: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the role of the BCR-ABL fusion protein and its tyrosine kinase activity in driving uncontrolled cell proliferation. (本题考察慢性髓性白血病 (CML) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCR-ABL 融合蛋白及其酪氨酸激酶活性在驱动细胞不受控制增殖中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety over the past 3 months. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any significant family history of hematologic malignancies. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a markedly elevated white blood cell (WBC) count of 180,000/µL with a left shift, including numerous immature granulocytes (myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands, and blasts). The red blood cell count and platelet count are also elevated. A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. The bone marrow aspirate confirms hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia, and cytogenetic analysis reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) confirms the presence of the BCR-ABL fusion gene. Which of the following molecular mechanisms is directly responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to mutations in JAK2.
(B) Constitutive activation of tyrosine kinase activity due to the formation of a fusion protein.
(C) Inhibition of apoptosis mediated by overexpression of BCL-2.
(D) Dysregulation of cell cycle checkpoints caused by mutations in p53.
(E) Aberrant activation of the Wnt signaling pathway leading to β-catenin stabilization.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (constitutional symptoms suggestive of malignancy or chronic inflammation), and splenomegaly (suggestive of extramedullary hematopoiesis or infiltration).
- **Laboratory Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180,000/µL) with a left shift (presence of immature granulocytes), elevated RBC count, and elevated platelet count. This constellation points towards a myeloproliferative neoplasm. The presence of immature granulocytes (myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands, blasts) is characteristic of CML.
- **Diagnostic Studies:** Bone marrow hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia confirms the myeloproliferative nature. Cytogenetic analysis showing the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)(q34;q11)) and FISH confirming the BCR-ABL fusion gene are pathognomonic for Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *molecular mechanism* directly responsible for the pathogenesis of CML, given the diagnosis confirmed by the clinical and laboratory findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome) results from a reciprocal translocation between the long arms of chromosomes 9 and 22, denoted as t(9;22)(q34;q11).
- This translocation fuses the *ABL1* gene (Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1) from chromosome 9 with the *BCR* gene (breakpoint cluster region) on chromosome 22.
- The resulting *BCR-ABL* fusion gene encodes a novel protein, the BCR-ABL fusion protein.
- The ABL1 gene normally encodes a non-receptor tyrosine kinase involved in cell growth, differentiation, and survival.
- The BCR portion of the fusion protein contains a myristoylation sequence, which anchors the BCR-ABL protein to the plasma membrane.
- The ABL kinase domain is constitutively active due to the fusion with BCR, meaning it phosphorylates downstream substrates continuously, independent of normal regulatory signals.
- This constitutive tyrosine kinase activity leads to uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells, inhibition of apoptosis, and genomic instability, driving the pathogenesis of CML.
- Targeted therapies for CML, such as imatinib (Gleevec), specifically inhibit the tyrosine kinase activity of the BCR-ABL protein.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to mutations in JAK2:** This mechanism is characteristic of other myeloproliferative neoplasms, primarily Polycythemia Vera (PV), Essential Thrombocythemia (ET), and Primary Myelofibrosis (PMF). While these conditions share some features with CML (e.g., elevated blood counts, splenomegaly), the underlying genetic abnormality (JAK2 V617F mutation or other JAK2 mutations) and the specific pathway activated (JAK-STAT) are different from CML. The patient's presentation and the presence of the Ph chromosome rule out these JAK2-mutated MPNs.
- (C) **Inhibition of apoptosis mediated by overexpression of BCL-2:** Overexpression of BCL-2, an anti-apoptotic protein, is a hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma, often due to the t(14;18) translocation involving the *BCL2* gene. While BCR-ABL does inhibit apoptosis, the primary mechanism is not BCL-2 overexpression; it's the constitutive tyrosine kinase activity affecting multiple downstream pathways involved in cell survival and proliferation.
- (D) **Dysregulation of cell cycle checkpoints caused by mutations in p53:** Mutations in the *TP53* gene (encoding p53) are common in many cancers, particularly acute myeloid leukemia (AML) and solid tumors. p53 is a tumor suppressor protein crucial for maintaining genomic stability and regulating cell cycle arrest and apoptosis. While CML cells can eventually develop p53 mutations during disease progression, the *initiating* molecular event and primary driver of pathogenesis is the BCR-ABL fusion protein, not a primary p53 mutation.
- (E) **Aberrant activation of the Wnt signaling pathway leading to β-catenin stabilization:** Activation of the Wnt pathway, often through mutations in *CTNNB1* (encoding β-catenin) or *APC*, is characteristic of Familial Adenomatous Polyposis (FAP) and many colorectal cancers. While Wnt signaling can be involved in hematopoiesis, it is not the primary driver of CML pathogenesis. The key driver in CML is the BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | Polycythemia Vera (PV) | Follicular Lymphoma | Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) | Colorectal Cancer (Wnt pathway) |
|----------------------|---------------------------------|--------------------------|----------------------|------------------------------|---------------------------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22) (Ph chromosome), BCR-ABL | JAK2 V617F mutation     | t(14;18), BCL2 overexpression | Variable (e.g., FLT3, NPM1, TP53) | APC, CTNNB1 mutations          |
| **Primary Molecular Mechanism** | Constitutive Tyrosine Kinase Activity | JAK-STAT pathway activation | BCL-2 mediated anti-apoptosis | Variable (often cell cycle dysregulation, apoptosis inhibition) | Wnt/β-catenin pathway activation |
| **Hallmark Cell Type** | Myeloid precursors (granulocytes) | Erythroid precursors     | Lymphocytes (centrocytes, centroblasts) | Immature myeloid blasts        | Epithelial cells                 |
| **Typical Presentation** | Elevated WBCs (left shift), splenomegaly | Elevated RBCs, thrombocytosis | Lymphadenopathy, indolent course | Acute onset, bone marrow failure | Colonic polyps, bowel changes |
| **Treatment Target** | BCR-ABL Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors | JAK inhibitors, phlebotomy | Anti-CD20 antibodies, chemo | Chemotherapy                  | Surgery, chemotherapy, targeted therapy |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The hallmark of CML is the Philadelphia chromosome resulting in the BCR-ABL fusion protein, which possesses constitutive tyrosine kinase activity, driving uncontrolled myeloid proliferation. Differentiating CML from other myeloproliferative neoplasms (like PV, ET, PMF) requires recognizing the specific genetic abnormality (Ph chromosome vs. JAK2 mutations) and the resulting molecular pathway (BCR-ABL tyrosine kinase vs. JAK-STAT). Remember that BCR-ABL is the specific target for CML therapy (e.g., imatinib).

<a id='question-059'></a>

### Question 059: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-09 (is seen with viral infections.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the pathogenesis of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically the role of the MYC oncogene translocation and its association with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection. (考察伯基特淋巴瘤的发病机制，特别是MYC癌基因易位的作用及其与EB病毒感染的关系。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass on his jaw, causing facial asymmetry and difficulty chewing. He reports a 2-week history of fever, night sweats, and weight loss. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass measuring approximately 10 cm in diameter on the right mandible. Lymphadenopathy is noted in the cervical and axillary regions. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1500 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5 and CD23. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14, t(8;14)(q24;q32). The patient is also positive for Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) antibodies. Which of the following molecular events is most directly responsible for the uncontrolled cell proliferation observed in this patient?

(A) Activation of the RAS signaling pathway due to a point mutation in the KRAS gene.
(B) Overexpression of the BCL2 protein due to a translocation involving the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18.
(C) Constitutive activation of the MYC transcription factor due to its translocation to an immunoglobulin gene locus.
(D) Inhibition of the tumor suppressor protein p53 due to a mutation in the TP53 gene.
(E) Aberrant activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to a mutation in the JAK2 gene.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa. This demographic is classic for endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with EBV.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass, fever, night sweats, weight loss. These are "B symptoms" and rapid growth characteristic of high-grade lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma. Jaw involvement is typical for the endemic form.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia. High LDH indicates high cell turnover, common in aggressive lymphomas. Hyperuricemia results from massive cell lysis.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with specific nuclear features (round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm) - classic "starry sky" appearance (though not explicitly mentioned, the cell description fits).
- **Immunophenotype:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD10+ (germinal center marker), CD5-, CD23-. This profile is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(8;14)(q24;q32). This is the hallmark translocation in ~85% of Burkitt lymphoma cases, placing the MYC oncogene under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain promoter.
- **EBV Status:** Positive. EBV is strongly associated with endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event directly responsible for uncontrolled proliferation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The patient presents with classic features of endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
- The key diagnostic finding is the t(8;14) translocation.
- This translocation juxtaposes the MYC proto-oncogene (located at 8q24) with the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus (located at 14q32).
- The IgH locus contains highly active enhancers that drive transcription.
- By placing MYC under the control of these IgH enhancers, the translocation leads to constitutive and markedly increased transcription and overexpression of the MYC protein.
- MYC is a transcription factor that regulates the expression of numerous genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, apoptosis, and metabolism.
- Overexpression of MYC drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, which is the hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma.
- EBV infection is a known risk factor, particularly for endemic Burkitt lymphoma, and can contribute to B-cell immortalization and proliferation, often synergizing with MYC dysregulation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Activation of the RAS signaling pathway due to a point mutation in the KRAS gene.** KRAS mutations are common in many cancers (e.g., pancreatic, colorectal, lung), activating the RAS/MAPK pathway promoting cell growth. However, KRAS mutations are not characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma. The primary driver in Burkitt lymphoma is MYC dysregulation.
- (B) **Overexpression of the BCL2 protein due to a translocation involving the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18.** The t(14;18) translocation, involving BCL2 and the IgH locus, is characteristic of follicular lymphoma, leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis. This is not the mechanism in Burkitt lymphoma.
- (C) **Constitutive activation of the MYC transcription factor due to its translocation to an immunoglobulin gene locus.** This accurately describes the mechanism driven by the t(8;14) translocation in Burkitt lymphoma. The MYC gene is placed under the control of strong IgH enhancers, leading to its overexpression and driving uncontrolled proliferation. This is the correct answer.
- (D) **Inhibition of the tumor suppressor protein p53 due to a mutation in the TP53 gene.** TP53 mutations are very common in many cancers (e.g., Li-Fraumeni syndrome, many sporadic cancers) and lead to loss of cell cycle control and apoptosis. While p53 function might be indirectly affected in Burkitt lymphoma, the primary driver is MYC overexpression, not direct p53 inhibition via TP53 mutation.
- (E) **Aberrant activation of the JAK-STAT pathway due to a mutation in the JAK2 gene.** JAK2 mutations (e.g., V617F) are characteristic of myeloproliferative neoplasms like polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, and primary myelofibrosis, leading to constitutive activation of JAK-STAT signaling. This is unrelated to Burkitt lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Endemic) | Follicular Lymphoma | Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL) | Myeloproliferative Neoplasms (e.g., PV) |
|----------------------|---------------------------|----------------------|-----------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
| **Primary Driver**   | t(8;14) -> MYC overexpression | t(14;18) -> BCL2 overexpression | Variable (e.g., deletions, mutations) | JAK2 mutation (e.g., V617F)             |
| **Cell Type**        | B-cell (Germinal Center)   | B-cell (Germinal Center) | B-cell (Mature)                   | Myeloid progenitor cells               |
| **Typical Location** | Jaw, abdomen (endemic)    | Lymph nodes          | Blood, lymph nodes, spleen         | Bone marrow                             |
| **Key Cytogenetic**  | t(8;14)                    | t(14;18)             | del(13q), del(11q), trisomy 12     | JAK2 V617F                             |
| **Key Immunophenotype**| CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, Ki-67 high | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+ | CD5+, CD19+, CD20+ (dim), CD23+     | Variable, often CD34+                  |
| **EBV Association** | Strong (Endemic)           | Weak                 | Rare                               | None                                   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by the t(8;14) translocation leading to MYC overexpression, driving rapid proliferation. Remember to differentiate it from follicular lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2 overexpression) and other B-cell lymphomas based on cytogenetics, immunophenotype, and clinical presentation. EBV association is key for endemic Burkitt lymphoma.
