<a id='question-070'></a>

### Question 070: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-20 (Chronic nonspecific lymphadenitiscauses nontendere)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL), specifically the role of BCL-2 in inhibiting apoptosis and its relevance to the accumulation of neoplastic lymphocytes. (问题考察慢性淋巴细胞白血病 (CLL) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCL-2 在抑制细胞凋亡中的作用，以及其与肿瘤淋巴细胞积累的相关性。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue and generalized malaise for the past 3 months. He has a history of hypertension managed with lisinopril and hyperlipidemia treated with atorvastatin. Physical examination reveals generalized, non-tender lymphadenopathy, particularly prominent in the cervical and axillary regions. Splenomegaly is also noted on abdominal examination. Initial laboratory tests show a white blood cell count of 65,000/µL with a predominance of small, mature-appearing lymphocytes. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals clonal expression of CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23. Cytogenetic analysis shows a deletion in the short arm of chromosome 13 (del(13q)). The patient is diagnosed with Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL). Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most directly responsible for the accumulation of neoplastic lymphocytes in this patient?

(A) Gain-of-function mutation in the *RAS* oncogene leading to constitutive activation of downstream signaling pathways promoting cell proliferation.
(B) Loss-of-function mutation in the *TP53* tumor suppressor gene resulting in impaired DNA damage response and increased genomic instability.
(C) Overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL-2 due to its translocation to the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus, inhibiting programmed cell death.
(D) Activation of the *MYC* oncogene through chromosomal translocation, driving increased cell cycle progression and metabolic activity.
(E) Loss-of-function mutation in the *ATM* gene leading to defective DNA repair mechanisms and increased susceptibility to apoptosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 68-year-old male with fatigue, malaise, generalized non-tender lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. These symptoms are classic for CLL.
- **Laboratory Findings:** High WBC count (65,000/µL) with lymphocyte predominance. This indicates a lymphoproliferative disorder.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Clonal expression of CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23. This immunophenotype is characteristic of CLL.
- **Cytogenetics:** Deletion in the short arm of chromosome 13 (del(13q)). This is a common cytogenetic abnormality in CLL, often associated with a better prognosis.
- **Diagnosis:** Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular mechanism responsible for the accumulation of neoplastic lymphocytes in CLL.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CLL Pathogenesis:** CLL is characterized by the accumulation of functionally incompetent, mature-appearing B lymphocytes. These cells are resistant to apoptosis, leading to their persistence and proliferation in the blood, bone marrow, and lymphoid tissues.
- **BCL-2 Overexpression:** A key mechanism underlying the resistance to apoptosis in CLL is the overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL-2. In the majority of CLL cases (~85%), this overexpression is caused by a translocation involving the *BCL2* gene on chromosome 18 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus on chromosome 14, t(14;18)(q32;q21). This translocation places the *BCL2* gene under the control of the strong IgH enhancer, leading to its constitutive overexpression.
- **BCL-2 Function:** BCL-2 is a member of the BCL-2 family of proteins that regulate apoptosis. It functions by inhibiting the release of cytochrome c from mitochondria, thereby preventing the activation of caspases and blocking the apoptotic pathway.
- **Accumulation of Lymphocytes:** The overexpression of BCL-2 prevents the normal programmed cell death (apoptosis) of these B lymphocytes, allowing them to accumulate in the peripheral blood and lymphoid organs, leading to the clinical manifestations of CLL.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Gain-of-function mutation in the *RAS* oncogene:** *RAS* mutations are common in many cancers, including some leukemias (e.g., AML), but they primarily drive cell proliferation by activating downstream signaling pathways like MAPK. While proliferation is involved in CLL, the primary defect is resistance to apoptosis, not constitutive proliferation driven by *RAS*. *RAS* mutations are not a characteristic feature of CLL pathogenesis.
- **(B) Loss-of-function mutation in the *TP53* tumor suppressor gene:** *TP53* mutations are found in a subset of CLL cases, particularly those with more aggressive disease or Richter's transformation. *TP53* loss impairs DNA damage response and can contribute to genomic instability and resistance to therapy, but it is not the primary mechanism responsible for the initial accumulation of CLL cells, which is primarily driven by BCL-2 overexpression.
- **(D) Activation of the *MYC* oncogene through chromosomal translocation:** *MYC* translocations, such as t(8;14) in Burkitt lymphoma, lead to constitutive *MYC* expression, driving rapid cell cycle progression and proliferation. While *MYC* can be involved in some lymphomas, it is not the characteristic driver of CLL pathogenesis. CLL cells are typically slow-growing and resistant to apoptosis, not characterized by rapid proliferation driven by *MYC*.
- **(E) Loss-of-function mutation in the *ATM* gene:** *ATM* is involved in DNA damage repair. Loss-of-function mutations can lead to genomic instability and increased sensitivity to DNA damaging agents. While defects in DNA repair can contribute to cancer development, *ATM* mutations are not a primary driver of CLL pathogenesis, and loss of *ATM* function would typically increase, not decrease, susceptibility to apoptosis in response to DNA damage.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | CLL (Correct Answer)                                  | RAS Oncogene Activation (A) | TP53 Loss (B)                     | MYC Activation (D)              | ATM Loss (E)                     |
|----------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------------------------|
| **Primary Mechanism** | Resistance to Apoptosis                               | Increased Proliferation      | Genomic Instability, Apoptosis Defect | Increased Proliferation, Metabolism | DNA Repair Defect, Apoptosis Sensitivity |
| **Key Molecule**     | BCL-2 Overexpression (t(14;18))                       | RAS Protein                 | p53 Protein                       | MYC Protein                      | ATM Protein                       |
| **Cell Fate**        | Accumulation of mature-appearing lymphocytes          | Uncontrolled Cell Division | Increased Mutation Rate           | Rapid Cell Division              | Increased DNA Damage Response     |
| **Typical Cancers**  | CLL, Follicular Lymphoma                              | AML, Pancreatic Cancer      | Li-Fraumeni Syndrome, CLL (subset) | Burkitt Lymphoma, Myc-driven Cancers | Ataxia-Telangiectasia, Cancer Predisposition |
| **Apoptosis Role**   | Inhibition of Apoptosis (Survival Signal)             | Indirect (Proliferation)    | Variable (Can affect apoptosis)   | Indirect (Proliferation)        | Increased Apoptosis Sensitivity   |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The accumulation of neoplastic cells in CLL is primarily driven by the overexpression of the anti-apoptotic BCL-2 protein, often due to the t(14;18) translocation. This prevents programmed cell death, leading to the characteristic lymphocytosis and lymphadenopathy. Remember to differentiate CLL's mechanism (apoptosis inhibition) from other leukemias/lymphomas driven by proliferation signals (e.g., RAS, MYC) or genomic instability (e.g., TP53).

<a id='question-071'></a>

### Question 071: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-21 (nodes. Follicular hyperplasia involves B lymphocyt)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of specific lymphomas, particularly the role of chromosomal translocations leading to oncogene activation and tumor suppressor gene inactivation, specifically focusing on the t(14;18) translocation in Follicular Lymphoma and its impact on BCL2 regulation. (理解特定淋巴瘤的分子发病机制，特别是染色体易位导致癌基因激活和抑癌基因失活的作用，具体关注滤泡性淋巴瘤中的t(14;18)易位及其对BCL2调节的影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old female presents to her primary care physician complaining of painless, progressive swelling in her neck and axillae over the past 6 months. She denies fever, night sweats, or weight loss (B symptoms). Her past medical history is significant for hypertension, well-controlled with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals multiple, firm, non-tender, rubbery lymph nodes in the cervical and axillary regions, ranging from 1 to 4 cm in diameter. The spleen is not palpable. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell count of 7,500/µL (normal range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a normal differential. Serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) is within normal limits. An excisional lymph node biopsy is performed. Histopathology reveals effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a proliferation of small, cleaved, and large centrocytes and centroblasts, forming nodules that are often back-to-back. Immunohistochemistry shows the neoplastic cells are positive for CD19, CD20, CD10, and BCL6, and negative for CD3 and CD15. Importantly, the neoplastic cells show strong positivity for BCL2 via immunohistochemistry. Which of the following molecular events is most likely responsible for the overexpression of BCL2 in this patient's lymphoma?

(A) Amplification of the *MYC* oncogene on chromosome 8.
(B) Translocation involving the *IGH* locus on chromosome 14 and the *BCL2* locus on chromosome 18.
(C) Mutation leading to constitutive activation of the *RAS* oncogene.
(D) Deletion of the *TP53* tumor suppressor gene on chromosome 17.
(E) Fusion of the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 with the *RARA* gene on chromosome 17.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old female with painless, progressive lymphadenopathy (neck, axillae) over 6 months, without B symptoms. This presentation is classic for indolent lymphoma.
*   **Physical Exam:** Multiple, firm, non-tender, rubbery lymph nodes. Spleen not palpable. Consistent with lymphoma, potentially follicular lymphoma given the indolent nature and node characteristics.
*   **Labs:** Normal CBC and LDH. This further supports an indolent lymphoma, as aggressive lymphomas often present with cytopenias or elevated LDH.
*   **Histopathology:** Effacement of normal architecture by nodules of small cleaved cells (centrocytes) and large cells (centroblasts), forming back-to-back nodules. This is the hallmark histological feature of Follicular Lymphoma.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+. CD19 and CD20 confirm B-cell lineage. CD10 and BCL6 are characteristic markers for germinal center B-cells, the origin of follicular lymphoma. Crucially, strong BCL2 positivity is a key diagnostic feature of follicular lymphoma.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event responsible for BCL2 overexpression.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **Follicular Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Follicular lymphoma is a B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma arising from germinal center B-cells. The defining genetic abnormality in the vast majority (>85%) of cases is the t(14;18)(q32;q21) chromosomal translocation.
*   **t(14;18) Mechanism:** This translocation juxtaposes the *IGH* (immunoglobulin heavy chain) locus on chromosome 14 with the *BCL2* locus on chromosome 18. The *IGH* locus contains enhancer elements that drive high-level transcription. This translocation places the *BCL2* gene under the control of the strong *IGH* enhancer, leading to its constitutive overexpression.
*   **BCL2 Function:** BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein. It inhibits apoptosis by preventing the release of cytochrome c from mitochondria, thereby blocking the activation of caspases.
*   **Consequence of BCL2 Overexpression:** Overexpression of BCL2 prevents programmed cell death (apoptosis) in the neoplastic germinal center B-cells, allowing them to accumulate and form the characteristic follicular lymphoma masses. This explains the indolent clinical course, as the cells are resistant to normal apoptotic signals.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) Amplification of the *MYC* oncogene on chromosome 8:** *MYC* amplification is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma, a highly aggressive B-cell lymphoma. Burkitt lymphoma typically presents with rapidly growing masses, often extranodal, and is associated with B symptoms and elevated LDH. The histology is different (starry sky appearance). This is incorrect for the indolent presentation and histology described.
*   **(C) Mutation leading to constitutive activation of the *RAS* oncogene:** *RAS* mutations are common in many cancers, including some leukemias (e.g., AML) and solid tumors, but are not the primary driver in follicular lymphoma. While RAS pathway activation can contribute to cell proliferation and survival, it is not the specific hallmark translocation associated with BCL2 overexpression in this context.
*   **(D) Deletion of the *TP53* tumor suppressor gene on chromosome 17:** *TP53* mutations or deletions are found in many cancers, often associated with aggressive behavior and resistance to therapy. While *TP53* mutations can occur in follicular lymphoma, particularly during transformation to a more aggressive lymphoma, they are not the primary initiating event responsible for BCL2 overexpression in the typical case.
*   **(E) Fusion of the *PML* gene on chromosome 15 with the *RARA* gene on chromosome 17:** The t(15;17) translocation creating the *PML-RARA* fusion gene is the defining genetic abnormality in Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML). APL presents acutely with pancytopenia, coagulopathy (DIC), and characteristic promyelocytes in the bone marrow and peripheral blood. This is completely different from the clinical presentation and histology of follicular lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18)) | Burkitt Lymphoma (*MYC* amp) | APL (t(15;17)) |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------- | :-------------------------- | :------------- |
| **Clinical Course**     | Indolent                     | Aggressive                 | Acute          |
| **Histology**           | Follicular pattern, centrocytes/centroblasts | Starry sky, diffuse | Promyelocytes |
| **Key Genetic Event**   | t(14;18) -> BCL2 overexpression | *MYC* amplification        | t(15;17) -> *PML-RARA* |
| **Key Protein**         | BCL2                         | MYC                        | PML-RARA       |
| **Primary Cell Type**   | Germinal Center B-cell        | Germinal Center B-cell     | Myeloid Progenitor |
| **Typical Presentation** | Painless lymphadenopathy      | Rapidly growing mass       | Acute leukemia |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(14;18) translocation juxtaposing the *IGH* and *BCL2* loci is the hallmark genetic abnormality in follicular lymphoma, leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis. Remember to differentiate follicular lymphoma (indolent, t(14;18), BCL2+) from Burkitt lymphoma (aggressive, *MYC* amp, starry sky) and APL (acute leukemia, t(15;17), *PML-RARA*).

<a id='question-072'></a>

### Question 072: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-22 (rheumatoid arthritis, toxoplasmosis, and early HIV)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the pathogenesis and molecular mechanisms of Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV)-associated lymphoproliferative disorders, specifically Burkitt lymphoma, in the context of immunosuppression (early HIV). (理解EBV相关淋巴增殖性疾病的发病机制和分子机制，特别是早期的HIV免疫抑制背景下的伯基特淋巴瘤。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old male with a history of rheumatoid arthritis, treated with methotrexate and prednisone for 5 years, presents with rapidly progressive swelling in his jaw and neck over the past month. He reports intermittent fevers, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss of 10 lbs. He denies any recent travel or exposure to sick individuals. His past medical history is significant for a positive HIV test 6 months ago, currently untreated. Physical examination reveals bilateral cervical lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, and a large, firm mass involving the mandible. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 850 U/L (normal < 250 U/L), hyperuricemia (uric acid 9.5 mg/dL, normal < 7.0 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9.5 g/dL, normal 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A biopsy of the mandibular mass shows a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages ("starry sky" appearance). Immunohistochemistry is positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. FISH analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8.

Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most directly responsible for the malignant transformation in this patient?

(A) Constitutive activation of the NF-κB pathway due to chronic inflammation from rheumatoid arthritis.
(B) Dysregulation of the BCL2 protein leading to inhibition of apoptosis.
(C) Aberrant activation of the MYC oncogene due to chromosomal translocation.
(D) Impaired T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against EBV-infected B-cells due to early HIV infection.
(E) Overexpression of the BCL6 transcription factor leading to germinal center B-cell differentiation block.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 28-year-old male with rheumatoid arthritis (RA) on immunosuppressants (methotrexate, prednisone) and newly diagnosed, untreated early HIV. This combination signifies significant immunosuppression.
- **Presenting Symptoms:** Rapidly progressive jaw/neck swelling, fevers, night sweats, weight loss – classic "B symptoms" suggestive of aggressive lymphoma. Jaw involvement is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Physical Exam:** Cervical lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly, mandibular mass – consistent with widespread lymphoproliferation.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH (high cell turnover/lysis), hyperuricemia (tumor lysis syndrome), anemia (bone marrow involvement or chronic disease) – support aggressive malignancy.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, "starry sky" appearance (macrophages phagocytosing apoptotic tumor cells) – classic morphology of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD10+ (germinal center marker), BCL6+ (germinal center marker), MUM1+ (plasma cell differentiation marker, often positive in Burkitt), CD5- (rules out mantle cell lymphoma), BCL2- (rules out follicular lymphoma) – immunophenotype consistent with Burkitt lymphoma.
- **FISH:** Translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 – confirms the genetic hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most direct* molecular mechanism responsible for malignant transformation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The patient presents with clinical, histological, and molecular features strongly indicative of Burkitt lymphoma.
- Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma.
- Its pathogenesis is intimately linked to the MYC oncogene.
- The characteristic genetic abnormality in Burkitt lymphoma is a chromosomal translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8. The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), which places MYC under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) enhancer on chromosome 14. Less common translocations involve MYC and the immunoglobulin light chain genes on chromosomes 2 (t(2;8)) or 22 (t(8;22)).
- These translocations lead to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein.
- MYC is a transcription factor that regulates cell growth, proliferation, differentiation, and apoptosis. Its overexpression drives uncontrolled cell proliferation, which is the hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma.
- The patient's immunosuppression (RA treatment + early HIV) increases the risk of EBV infection and subsequent EBV-driven lymphoproliferation, including Burkitt lymphoma. EBV infection is strongly associated with endemic Burkitt lymphoma, but can also occur in sporadic or immunodeficiency-associated cases. While EBV plays a role in the pathogenesis, the *direct* driver of malignant transformation is MYC overexpression due to the translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Constitutive activation of the NF-κB pathway due to chronic inflammation from rheumatoid arthritis.** NF-κB activation is crucial in the pathogenesis of several lymphomas, particularly diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL) and MALT lymphoma, often associated with chronic inflammation or H. pylori infection. While chronic inflammation can contribute to lymphomagenesis, it is not the primary driver in Burkitt lymphoma, which is characterized by MYC translocation. The specific genetic hallmark (MYC translocation) points away from NF-κB as the *most direct* cause.
- (B) **Dysregulation of the BCL2 protein leading to inhibition of apoptosis.** BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein. Its overexpression, typically due to the t(14;18) translocation involving the BCL2 gene and the IGH locus, is the hallmark of follicular lymphoma. The patient's immunohistochemistry is BCL2 negative, and the morphology/genetics are inconsistent with follicular lymphoma. While apoptosis is dysregulated in Burkitt lymphoma, the primary mechanism is MYC overexpression, not BCL2 overexpression.
- (D) **Impaired T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against EBV-infected B-cells due to early HIV infection.** Early HIV infection leads to impaired cell-mediated immunity, including reduced cytotoxic T lymphocyte (CTL) function. This allows EBV-infected B-cells to escape immune surveillance and proliferate, increasing the risk of EBV-associated lymphomas, including Burkitt lymphoma. However, this describes the *predisposing factor* (immunosuppression facilitating EBV proliferation) rather than the *direct molecular mechanism* of malignant transformation itself, which is MYC overexpression.
- (E) **Overexpression of the BCL6 transcription factor leading to germinal center B-cell differentiation block.** BCL6 is a transcription factor essential for germinal center formation and function. It is normally expressed in germinal center B-cells and is typically positive in Burkitt lymphoma (as seen in the vignette). However, BCL6 overexpression itself is not the primary oncogenic driver. The MYC translocation is the critical event leading to uncontrolled proliferation. While BCL6 is involved in the cell type of origin, MYC is the direct oncogene driving the malignancy.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma (Correct Answer) | Follicular Lymphoma | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma |
|----------------------|------------------------------------|----------------------|---------------------------------------|----------------------|
| **Key Genetic Event** | t(8;14), t(2;8), or t(8;22) (MYC) | t(14;18) (BCL2)      | Variable (often MYC, BCL2, BCL6, TP53) | t(11;14) (CCND1)      |
| **Primary Driver**    | MYC overexpression                 | BCL2 overexpression  | Variable                             | CCND1 overexpression  |
| **Morphology**        | "Starry sky"                       | Follicular pattern   | Diffuse sheets                        | Nodal/diffuse        |
| **Immunophenotype**   | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+ | Variable (CD20+, BCL6+, MUM1+)        | CD20+, CD5+, Cyclin D1+ |
| **Clinical**         | Rapid growth, jaw involvement      | Indolent, widespread | Aggressive                            | Variable             |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by MYC translocation leading to MYC overexpression and uncontrolled proliferation. Differentiate it from other lymphomas based on morphology ("starry sky"), immunophenotype (CD10+, BCL2-), and specific genetic translocations (MYC vs. BCL2 vs. CCND1). Understand the role of immunosuppression (HIV, RA treatment) as a risk factor for EBV-associated lymphomas like Burkitt lymphoma, but recognize that the *direct* oncogenic mechanism is the MYC translocation.

<a id='question-073'></a>

### Question 073: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-23 (lymphoid hyperplasia involves T cells and may be s)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of specific lymphoid malignancies, particularly the role of chromosomal translocations and resulting fusion proteins in driving leukemogenesis. (理解特定淋巴细胞恶性肿瘤的分子发病机制，特别是染色体易位和由此产生的融合蛋白在驱动白血病发生发展中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of worsening fatigue, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss (10 lbs). His past medical history is significant for hypertension treated with lisinopril and hyperlipidemia treated with atorvastatin. He denies any history of smoking, alcohol abuse, or illicit drug use. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, including cervical, axillary, and inguinal nodes, which are firm and non-tender. Splenomegaly is also noted on abdominal examination. Initial laboratory investigations show a white blood cell count of 185,000/µL with a predominance of mature-appearing lymphocytes, hemoglobin of 9.5 g/dL, and platelet count of 45,000/µL. A peripheral blood smear shows numerous lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and inconspicuous nucleoli. Flow cytometry reveals the lymphocytes to be CD5+, CD19+, CD20+, CD23+, and surface immunoglobulin (sIg) positive. Cytogenetic analysis of bone marrow aspirate reveals the t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation.

Which of the following molecular targets is most likely involved in the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) BCR-ABL1 fusion protein
(B) MYC oncogene translocation
(C) CD5 protein overexpression
(D) Cyclin D1 overexpression
(E) T-cell receptor (TCR) gene rearrangement

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), generalized lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. This constellation suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder.
*   **Laboratory Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (185,000/µL) with lymphocyte predominance, anemia (Hgb 9.5 g/dL), and thrombocytopenia (Plt 45,000/µL). This indicates bone marrow infiltration by malignant lymphocytes, leading to cytopenias.
*   **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and inconspicuous nucleoli are characteristic of small lymphocytic lymphoma (SLL) or chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL).
*   **Flow Cytometry:** CD5+, CD19+, CD20+, CD23+, sIg+ phenotype is classic for CLL/SLL. CD5 is typically a T-cell marker but is aberrantly expressed in CLL/SLL B cells. CD19, CD20, and sIg confirm B-cell lineage. CD23 positivity is also characteristic.
*   **Cytogenetics:** t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation. This specific translocation involves the *CCND1* gene (encoding Cyclin D1) on chromosome 11 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IgH) locus on chromosome 14.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular target involved in the pathogenesis, given the clinical and cytogenetic findings consistent with CLL/SLL and the specific t(11;14) translocation.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   The t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation places the *CCND1* gene (Cyclin D1) under the control of the strong IgH enhancer.
*   This leads to overexpression of Cyclin D1 protein in the B cells.
*   Cyclin D1 forms a complex with Cyclin-Dependent Kinase 4 (CDK4) or CDK6.
*   The Cyclin D1/CDK4/6 complex phosphorylates the retinoblastoma protein (pRb).
*   Phosphorylation of pRb releases the E2F transcription factor.
*   E2F promotes the transcription of genes required for cell cycle progression from G1 to S phase.
*   Overexpression of Cyclin D1 leads to constitutive activation of the G1/S checkpoint, promoting uncontrolled proliferation of the B cells, characteristic of CLL/SLL.
*   Therefore, Cyclin D1 overexpression is the key molecular event driving the pathogenesis in this specific subset of CLL/SLL patients with the t(11;14) translocation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
*   **(A) BCR-ABL1 fusion protein:** This is the hallmark of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), resulting from the t(9;22)(q34;q11) translocation (Philadelphia chromosome). It leads to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity, driving myeloid proliferation. This patient has a B-cell phenotype (CD19+, CD20+, sIg+) and the t(11;14) translocation, not t(9;22).
*   **(B) MYC oncogene translocation:** Translocations involving the *MYC* gene (e.g., t(8;14) in Burkitt lymphoma, t(2;8) in mantle cell lymphoma) lead to *MYC* overexpression, a potent oncogene driving cell proliferation. While *MYC* is involved in lymphoma pathogenesis, it is not directly associated with the t(11;14) translocation seen in this patient.
*   **(C) CD5 protein overexpression:** CD5 is aberrantly expressed in CLL/SLL B cells, but it is a surface marker, not the primary molecular target driving the disease pathogenesis resulting from the t(11;14) translocation. The translocation itself leads to Cyclin D1 overexpression. While CD5 is part of the phenotype, it's not the *target* of the translocation's effect.
*   **(E) T-cell receptor (TCR) gene rearrangement:** TCR gene rearrangements are characteristic of T-cell lymphomas and leukemias. This patient has a B-cell phenotype (CD19+, CD20+, sIg+), making TCR rearrangement unlikely as the primary pathogenic event.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | CLL/SLL with t(11;14) | CML (t(9;22)) | Burkitt Lymphoma (t(8;14)) | Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(2;8)) | T-cell Lymphoma |
| :---------------------- | :------------------- | :------------ | :------------------------ | :--------------------------- | :-------------- |
| **Cell Lineage**        | B-cell               | Myeloid       | B-cell                   | B-cell                      | T-cell          |
| **Key Translocation**   | t(11;14)             | t(9;22)       | t(8;14)                  | t(2;8)                      | Various         |
| **Fusion Protein/Gene** | Cyclin D1            | BCR-ABL1      | MYC                      | MYC                          | N/A             |
| **Key Molecular Target**| Cyclin D1/CDK4/6     | BCR-ABL1 TK   | MYC                      | MYC                          | TCR             |
| **Phenotype (Example)** | CD5+, CD19+, CD20+    | CD34+, CD117+ | CD19+, CD20+, CD10+       | CD5+, CD19+, CD20+           | CD3+, CD4/8+    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(11;14) translocation in CLL/SLL leads to Cyclin D1 overexpression, which drives uncontrolled cell cycle progression. Remember to associate specific chromosomal translocations with their resulting fusion proteins or gene overexpression and the corresponding diseases. Differentiate CLL/SLL from other B-cell lymphomas (like Burkitt or Mantle Cell) and myeloid neoplasms (like CML) based on cytogenetics, phenotype, and clinical presentation.

<a id='question-074'></a>

### Question 074: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-24 (tin), and systemic lupus erythematosus.Sinus histi)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the pathophysiology and molecular mechanisms underlying the development of secondary malignancies, specifically acute myeloid leukemia (AML), in the context of systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE) and its treatment, focusing on the role of DNA damage and repair pathways. (问题考察了系统性红斑狼疮 (SLE) 及其治疗背景下，继发恶性肿瘤，特别是急性髓系白血病 (AML) 的发病机制和分子机制，重点关注 DNA 损伤和修复通路的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 45-year-old female with a 15-year history of systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE) presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of worsening fatigue, fever, and easy bruising. Her SLE has been managed with long-term, low-dose prednisone (5 mg daily) and hydroxychloroquine. She denies recent infections or changes in her medications. Physical examination reveals pallor, petechiae on the lower extremities, and mild splenomegaly. Laboratory studies show pancytopenia (WBC 2.5 x 10^9/L, Hb 8.0 g/dL, Platelets 40 x 10^9/L), elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH 850 U/L), and hyperuricemia (uric acid 10 mg/dL). A peripheral blood smear shows dysplastic changes in myeloid precursors, including pseudo-Pelger-Huët anomaly and Auer rods. Bone marrow biopsy confirms the diagnosis of acute myeloid leukemia (AML) with 20% blasts. Cytogenetic analysis reveals a normal karyotype. Molecular testing reveals a mutation in the *TP53* gene.

Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most likely responsible for the development of AML in this patient?

(A) Aberrant activation of the JAK-STAT pathway leading to constitutive proliferation of hematopoietic stem cells.
(B) Impaired DNA double-strand break repair due to chronic exposure to immunosuppressive agents, resulting in genomic instability.
(C) Fusion gene formation involving the *BCR-ABL1* translocation, leading to constitutive tyrosine kinase activity.
(D) Defective apoptosis regulation due to overexpression of BCL-2, preventing programmed cell death of leukemic cells.
(E) Increased production of autoantibodies targeting hematopoietic progenitor cells, leading to bone marrow failure and subsequent leukemic transformation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 45-year-old female with long-standing SLE (15 years). This history is crucial as SLE itself and its treatments are associated with increased risk of certain malignancies.
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, fever, easy bruising (suggestive of cytopenias).
- **Medications:** Long-term low-dose prednisone and hydroxychloroquine. Hydroxychloroquine is generally considered protective against SLE flares but not directly linked to AML. Prednisone, especially long-term, can have immunosuppressive effects.
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), petechiae (thrombocytopenia), mild splenomegaly (common in SLE and hematologic malignancies).
- **Labs:** Pancytopenia (WBC, Hb, Platelets low), elevated LDH (cell turnover/damage), hyperuricemia (cell turnover). These findings point towards bone marrow failure or infiltration.
- **Peripheral Smear:** Dysplastic changes (pseudo-Pelger-Huët, Auer rods) strongly suggest myeloid lineage dysplasia and transformation to AML.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Confirms AML (20% blasts).
- **Cytogenetics:** Normal karyotype rules out common chromosomal translocations like t(8;21), t(15;17), inv(16), or t(11q23).
- **Molecular Testing:** *TP53* mutation. This is a key finding. *TP53* is a tumor suppressor gene involved in DNA damage response and apoptosis. Mutations in *TP53* are often associated with therapy-related AML (t-AML) and AML arising in the context of certain genetic syndromes or autoimmune diseases.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *most likely* molecular mechanism responsible for AML development in this specific context (long-standing SLE, potential treatment effects, *TP53* mutation).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **SLE and AML Risk:** Patients with SLE have an increased risk of developing AML, particularly therapy-related AML (t-AML). This risk is further elevated by the use of certain immunosuppressive agents.
- **Mechanism of t-AML:** Alkylating agents (like cyclophosphamide, sometimes used in severe SLE) and topoisomerase II inhibitors (like etoposide) can cause DNA damage. Chronic exposure to these agents, even at lower doses used for SLE management, can lead to genomic instability.
- **Role of DNA Repair:** Cells rely on DNA repair pathways, including those involving *TP53*, to fix DNA damage. If these pathways are compromised (e.g., by *TP53* mutation or chronic DNA damage overwhelming the repair capacity), mutations accumulate, increasing the risk of malignant transformation.
- ***TP53* Function:** The *TP53* gene encodes the p53 protein, a crucial transcription factor that acts as a "guardian of the genome." It responds to DNA damage by inducing cell cycle arrest (allowing time for repair), activating DNA repair genes, or triggering apoptosis if the damage is irreparable.
- ***TP53* Mutation in AML:** Mutations in *TP53* are common in t-AML and AML associated with certain genetic conditions (e.g., Li-Fraumeni syndrome). In this context, the *TP53* mutation impairs the cell's ability to respond effectively to DNA damage, leading to the accumulation of further mutations and genomic instability, ultimately driving leukemogenesis.
- **Connecting the Dots:** The patient has long-standing SLE, potentially exposed to DNA-damaging agents (even if not explicitly stated, immunosuppression for SLE often involves agents with this potential, or the chronic inflammation itself contributes to DNA damage). The presence of a *TP53* mutation in the resulting AML strongly suggests that impaired DNA damage response and repair played a critical role in the leukemic transformation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Aberrant activation of the JAK-STAT pathway is characteristic of myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPNs) like polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, and primary myelofibrosis. While JAK2 mutations can occur in AML, they are not the primary mechanism associated with t-AML in SLE patients with *TP53* mutations. This option describes a different class of hematopoietic disorders.
- (C): The *BCR-ABL1* fusion gene is the hallmark of chronic myeloid leukemia (CML) and a subset of acute lymphoblastic leukemia (ALL). It results from the Philadelphia chromosome translocation t(9;22). This is unrelated to the pathogenesis of AML in SLE patients, especially those with *TP53* mutations and normal karyotypes.
- (D): Overexpression of BCL-2, an anti-apoptotic protein, is characteristic of follicular lymphoma due to the t(14;18) translocation. While BCL-2 dysregulation can occur in AML, it's not the primary mechanism linked to *TP53* mutations and therapy-related leukemogenesis in SLE. The *TP53* mutation itself impairs apoptosis induction, making this option less specific and less likely than impaired DNA repair.
- (E): Autoantibodies targeting hematopoietic progenitors are central to the pathogenesis of autoimmune cytopenias, which can occur in SLE. However, while chronic immune stimulation and inflammation might contribute to genomic instability, autoantibodies are not considered the direct cause of leukemic transformation in this scenario, especially given the identified *TP53* mutation pointing towards a DNA damage/repair defect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (B)                                  | Option (A) JAK-STAT Activation                 | Option (C) BCR-ABL1 Fusion                  | Option (D) BCL-2 Overexpression             | Option (E) Autoantibody Targeting          |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------- | :----------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------- |
| **Pathophysiology**     | Impaired DNA repair -> Genomic instability -> AML    | Constitutive proliferation                   | Constitutive tyrosine kinase activity      | Impaired apoptosis                         | Immune-mediated bone marrow suppression |
| **Associated Disease**  | Therapy-related AML, SLE-associated AML             | Myeloproliferative Neoplasms (MPNs)          | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), ALL         | Follicular Lymphoma                      | Autoimmune Cytopenias (SLE)              |
| **Key Molecular Event** | *TP53* mutation, DNA damage response defect         | *JAK2*, *MPL*, *CALR* mutations               | t(9;22) translocation                      | t(14;18) translocation                    | Autoantibody production                  |
| **Clinical Context**    | Long-term SLE, potential immunosuppression, *TP53*+ | Primary bone marrow disorder                  | Specific chromosomal translocation         | Specific chromosomal translocation        | Autoimmune disease                      |
| **Relevance to Vignette**| High (SLE, *TP53* mutation)                        | Low                                           | Low                                        | Low                                       | Low (indirect contribution possible)      |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Long-term immunosuppression for autoimmune diseases like SLE increases the risk of therapy-related AML (t-AML). t-AML is often associated with genomic instability and mutations in DNA damage response genes, most commonly *TP53*. Understand the role of *TP53* in DNA repair and apoptosis and how its mutation contributes to leukemogenesis in this context. Differentiate t-AML mechanisms from those of primary leukemias (e.g., *BCR-ABL1* in CML) or other lymphoid malignancies (e.g., *BCL2* in follicular lymphoma).

<a id='question-075'></a>

### Question 075: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-25 (and, in most cases, is nonspecific; an example is )
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of specific leukemias, particularly the role of chromosomal translocations leading to fusion proteins that drive oncogenesis. (理解特定白血病的分子发病机制，特别是染色体易位导致融合蛋白驱动癌基因形成的机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-week history of worsening fatigue, night sweats, and bone pain. His past medical history is significant for hypertension treated with lisinopril and hyperlipidemia treated with atorvastatin. He denies any history of smoking, alcohol abuse, or illicit drug use. Physical examination reveals pallor, hepatosplenomegaly, and multiple ecchymoses on his extremities. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 180 x 10^9/L (normal range: 4-11 x 10^9/L) with a predominance of immature myeloid cells (blasts, promyelocytes, myelocytes). Hemoglobin is 7.5 g/dL (normal range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelet count is 20 x 10^9/L (normal range: 150-400 x 10^9/L). A bone marrow biopsy shows hypercellularity with marked myeloid hyperplasia and infiltration by blasts. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the t(15;17) translocation.

Which of the following molecular events is most likely responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein, leading to constitutive activation of tyrosine kinase activity.
(B) Formation of the PML-RARA fusion protein, leading to aberrant retinoic acid signaling and myeloid differentiation arrest.
(C) Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein, leading to abnormal lymphoid development and B-cell precursor acute lymphoblastic leukemia.
(D) Formation of the MYC-BCL2 fusion protein, leading to inhibition of apoptosis and proliferation of mature B-cells.
(E) Formation of the FLT3-ITD mutation, leading to constitutive activation of the FLT3 receptor tyrosine kinase and increased proliferation of myeloid progenitors.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, bone pain (constitutional symptoms suggestive of malignancy).
- **Physical Exam:** Pallor (anemia), hepatosplenomegaly (extramedullary hematopoiesis or infiltration), ecchymoses (thrombocytopenia).
- **CBC:** Markedly elevated WBC count (leukocytosis) with a predominance of immature myeloid cells (blasts, promyelocytes, myelocytes) - indicates acute myeloid leukemia (AML). Anemia (low Hb) and thrombocytopenia (low platelets) are also present, consistent with bone marrow failure due to leukemic infiltration.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Hypercellularity with myeloid hyperplasia and blast infiltration confirms AML.
- **Cytogenetics:** t(15;17) translocation is the key diagnostic clue. This specific translocation is pathognomonic for Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL), a subtype of AML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event responsible for the pathogenesis, specifically linking the cytogenetic finding (t(15;17)) to its molecular consequence.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The t(15;17) translocation involves the exchange of genetic material between chromosome 15 and chromosome 17.
- Specifically, it fuses the *PML* gene (promyelocytic leukemia gene) on chromosome 15 with the *RARA* gene (retinoic acid receptor alpha gene) on chromosome 17.
- This fusion creates the *PML-RARA* fusion gene, which encodes the PML-RARA fusion protein.
- The PML-RARA fusion protein acts as an aberrant transcription factor. It binds to retinoic acid response elements (RAREs) on DNA but, unlike the normal RARA protein, it does not effectively recruit coactivators upon binding retinoic acid.
- This leads to the repression of genes required for myeloid differentiation, causing a block in the differentiation of promyelocytes into mature granulocytes.
- The accumulation of abnormal promyelocytes in the bone marrow and peripheral blood is the hallmark of APL.
- The PML-RARA fusion protein also recruits histone deacetylases (HDACs) and other corepressor complexes to chromatin, leading to transcriptional silencing of differentiation genes.
- Treatment with all-trans retinoic acid (ATRA) overcomes this block by binding to the PML-RARA fusion protein, inducing a conformational change that allows it to recruit coactivators and initiate myeloid differentiation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Formation of the BCR-ABL fusion protein is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). The t(9;22) translocation (Philadelphia chromosome) creates this fusion, resulting in a constitutively active tyrosine kinase that drives uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells. This is incorrect because the patient has t(15;17), not t(9;22).
- (C): Formation of the ETV6-RUNX1 fusion protein is characteristic of B-cell precursor ALL, particularly in children. The t(12;21) translocation creates this fusion, which disrupts normal B-cell development. This is incorrect because the patient has AML with myeloid blasts and the t(15;17) translocation.
- (D): Formation of the MYC-BCL2 fusion protein is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma. The t(14;18) translocation creates this fusion, leading to overexpression of the anti-apoptotic BCL2 protein and inhibition of apoptosis in germinal center B-cells. This is incorrect because the patient has AML, not lymphoma, and the translocation is t(15;17).
- (E): The FLT3-ITD (internal tandem duplication) mutation is a common mutation in AML, particularly in patients with normal cytogenetics or certain specific cytogenetic abnormalities (like t(8;21) or inv(16)). It leads to constitutive activation of the FLT3 receptor tyrosine kinase, promoting proliferation and survival of myeloid progenitors. While it can occur in AML, it is not the specific molecular consequence of the t(15;17) translocation found in this patient. The t(15;17) defines APL, which is caused by the PML-RARA fusion.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):

| Feature             | Acute Promyelocytic Leukemia (APL) | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | B-cell ALL (ETV6-RUNX1) | Follicular Lymphoma (MYC-BCL2) | AML (FLT3-ITD) |
|----------------------|------------------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------|---------------------------------|-----------------|
| **Key Cytogenetic** | t(15;17)                           | t(9;22)                        | t(12;21)                 | t(14;18)                        | FLT3-ITD        |
| **Fusion Protein**  | PML-RARA                           | BCR-ABL                       | ETV6-RUNX1               | MYC-BCL2                        | N/A (Mutation)  |
| **Cell Lineage**    | Myeloid (Promyelocytes)             | Myeloid                       | Lymphoid (B-cell)        | Lymphoid (B-cell)               | Myeloid         |
| **Pathophysiology** | Differentiation block, aberrant RA signaling | Constitutive tyrosine kinase activity | Disrupted B-cell development | Inhibition of apoptosis        | Constitutive TK activity |
| **Treatment**       | ATRA, Arsenic Trioxide              | Tyrosine Kinase Inhibitors (e.g., Imatinib) | Chemotherapy             | Chemotherapy, Rituximab        | Chemotherapy    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The t(15;17) translocation in APL leads to the formation of the PML-RARA fusion protein, which blocks myeloid differentiation. This is a classic example of how specific chromosomal translocations drive leukemia pathogenesis. Differentiating APL from other AML subtypes and leukemias based on cytogenetics and molecular findings is crucial for appropriate management (e.g., ATRA for APL). Key differentials include CML (t(9;22), BCR-ABL), B-ALL (t(12;21), ETV6-RUNX1), Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18), MYC-BCL2), and other AML subtypes (e.g., FLT3-ITD).

<a id='question-076'></a>

### Question 076: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-26 (Neoplastic Enlargement)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the role of the BCR-ABL fusion protein as a constitutively active tyrosine kinase, and its inhibition by tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs). (理解慢性粒细胞白血病 (CML) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCR-ABL 融合蛋白作为持续活性的酪氨酸激酶的作用，以及酪氨酸激酶抑制剂 (TKIs) 对其抑制作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety for the past 3 months. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals marked splenomegaly, extending 10 cm below the left costal margin, and mild hepatomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 180 x 10^9/L (reference range: 4-11 x 10^9/L) with a left shift, including numerous myelocytes, metamyelocytes, and bands. Hemoglobin is 10 g/dL (reference range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelet count is 600 x 10^9/L (reference range: 150-400 x 10^9/L). A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Molecular testing confirms the presence of the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene. The patient is started on imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor. Which of the following molecular targets is directly inhibited by imatinib in this patient?

(A) JAK2
(B) FLT3
(C) BCR-ABL1
(D) KIT
(E) ALK

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (constitutional symptoms), splenomegaly, hepatomegaly. These symptoms suggest a myeloproliferative neoplasm or lymphoma.
- **CBC Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180 x 10^9/L) with a left shift (immature granulocytes like myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands) indicates uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells. Anemia (Hgb 10 g/dL) and thrombocytosis (platelets 600 x 10^9/L) are also common in CML.
- **Diagnostic Tests:**
    - **Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11):** This is the hallmark cytogenetic abnormality of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML). It results from a reciprocal translocation between chromosomes 9 and 22.
    - **BCR-ABL1 fusion gene:** This gene is formed by the fusion of the breakpoint cluster region (BCR) gene on chromosome 22 and the Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 (ABL1) gene on chromosome 9. The translocation creates a fusion gene encoding a constitutively active tyrosine kinase protein (BCR-ABL1).
- **Treatment:** Imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI), is the standard first-line therapy for CML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific molecular target of imatinib in this patient with CML characterized by the Ph chromosome and BCR-ABL1 fusion gene.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CML Pathogenesis:** The t(9;22) translocation creates the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene. The resulting BCR-ABL1 protein is a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Unlike normal ABL1, which requires activation signals, BCR-ABL1 is always "on," leading to uncontrolled proliferation and survival of myeloid progenitor cells. This results in the characteristic leukocytosis with a left shift seen in CML.
- **Imatinib Mechanism:** Imatinib (Gleevec) is a small-molecule tyrosine kinase inhibitor specifically designed to target the ATP-binding site of the BCR-ABL1 protein. By binding to this site, imatinib prevents ATP from binding and phosphorylating downstream substrates, thereby inhibiting the kinase activity of BCR-ABL1. This blocks the signaling pathways responsible for the uncontrolled proliferation and survival of CML cells, leading to disease remission.
- **Other Options:**
    - **JAK2:** A tyrosine kinase involved in cytokine receptor signaling. Mutations (e.g., V617F) are common in polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, and primary myelofibrosis. Not the target in CML.
    - **FLT3:** A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in hematopoiesis. Mutations (e.g., ITD, TKD) are common in acute myeloid leukemia (AML), particularly cytogenetically normal AML. Not the target in CML.
    - **KIT:** A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in hematopoiesis and melanogenesis. Mutations are associated with gastrointestinal stromal tumors (GISTs), mastocytosis, and some AML cases. Not the target in CML.
    - **ALK:** A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in neuronal development. Rearrangements (e.g., EML4-ALK) are associated with non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) and anaplastic large cell lymphoma (ALCL). Not the target in CML.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **JAK2:** Incorrect. JAK2 mutations are characteristic of other myeloproliferative neoplasms like polycythemia vera and essential thrombocythemia, not CML. The hallmark of CML is the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene.
- (B) **FLT3:** Incorrect. FLT3 mutations are primarily associated with acute myeloid leukemia (AML), not CML. CML is a chronic myeloproliferative neoplasm characterized by the Ph chromosome.
- (D) **KIT:** Incorrect. KIT mutations are associated with GISTs, mastocytosis, and some AML subtypes. They are not the driving force or therapeutic target in CML.
- (E) **ALK:** Incorrect. ALK rearrangements are primarily associated with certain types of lung cancer and lymphoma, not CML.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | CML (Philadelphia Chromosome Positive) | Polycythemia Vera (PV) | Essential Thrombocythemia (ET) | Primary Myelofibrosis (PMF) | AML (FLT3-ITD) |
|----------------------|----------------------------------------|------------------------|-------------------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22) -> BCR-ABL1 fusion           | JAK2 V617F mutation   | JAK2 V617F mutation          | JAK2/CALR/MPL mutations     | FLT3-ITD mutation |
| **Primary Cell Line Affected** | Myeloid progenitor cells            | Erythroid progenitor   | Megakaryocytic progenitor    | Megakaryocytic progenitor  | Myeloid blasts   |
| **Typical CBC Findings** | Leukocytosis (left shift), basophilia, thrombocytosis, anemia | Erythrocytosis, leukocytosis, thrombocytosis | Thrombocytosis, leukocytosis | Anemia, leukoerythroblastic picture, teardrop cells | Blasts, cytopenias |
| **Splenomegaly**      | Common, often marked                  | Common                 | Common                        | Common                      | Variable         |
| **Molecular Target for Therapy** | BCR-ABL1 (e.g., Imatinib)           | JAK2 (e.g., Ruxolitinib) | JAK2 (e.g., Ruxolitinib)      | JAK2/CALR/MPL (e.g., Ruxolitinib) | FLT3 (e.g., Midostaurin) |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The hallmark of CML is the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)), leading to the BCR-ABL1 fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Imatinib is a targeted therapy that specifically inhibits BCR-ABL1. Differentiating CML from other myeloproliferative neoplasms (MPNs) like PV, ET, and PMF often involves recognizing the specific genetic mutations (JAK2, CALR, MPL) and clinical/hematologic features. AML is an acute leukemia characterized by blast proliferation, distinct from the chronic phase of CML. Remember the specific molecular targets for therapies in these different hematologic malignancies.

<a id='question-077'></a>

### Question 077: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-27 (Neoplasia usually causes nontender enlargement of lymph nodes)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the role of the BCR-ABL fusion protein as a constitutively active tyrosine kinase, and its inhibition by tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs). (理解慢性粒细胞白血病 (CML) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCR-ABL 融合蛋白作为持续活性的酪氨酸激酶的作用，以及酪氨酸激酶抑制剂 (TKIs) 对其抑制作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety for the past 3 months. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals marked splenomegaly, extending 8 cm below the left costal margin, and mild hepatomegaly. There is no lymphadenopathy or significant peripheral lymphocytosis. Complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 180 x 10^9/L (reference range: 4.0-11.0 x 10^9/L) with a left shift, including numerous myelocytes, metamyelocytes, and bands. Hemoglobin is 10.5 g/dL (reference range: 13.5-17.5 g/dL), and platelet count is 650 x 10^9/L (reference range: 150-400 x 10^9/L). A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Molecular analysis confirms the presence of the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene. The patient is started on imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor. Which of the following molecular targets is inhibited by imatinib in this patient?

(A) JAK2
(B) FLT3
(C) BCR-ABL1
(D) KIT
(E) ALK

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 52-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (constitutional symptoms suggestive of malignancy or chronic inflammation).
- **Physical Exam:** Marked splenomegaly (classic finding in CML due to extramedullary hematopoiesis and infiltration), mild hepatomegaly. Absence of lymphadenopathy and significant peripheral lymphocytosis helps differentiate from certain leukemias/lymphomas.
- **CBC:** High WBC count (leukocytosis) with a left shift (immature granulocytes in peripheral blood), anemia (low Hb), and thrombocytosis (high platelets). This pattern is characteristic of CML.
- **Cytogenetics:** Philadelphia chromosome (Ph), t(9;22)(q34;q11), is the hallmark genetic abnormality in CML.
- **Molecular Analysis:** Confirms the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene, resulting from the t(9;22) translocation. This fusion gene encodes a constitutively active tyrosine kinase.
- **Treatment:** Imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI), is the standard first-line therapy for CML.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific molecular target of imatinib in this patient with CML characterized by the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CML Pathogenesis:** CML is a myeloproliferative neoplasm characterized by the uncontrolled proliferation of myeloid cells. The underlying cause is the t(9;22) translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome. This translocation fuses the ABL1 gene (a proto-oncogene encoding a non-receptor tyrosine kinase) on chromosome 9 with the BCR gene on chromosome 22.
- **BCR-ABL1 Fusion Protein:** The resulting BCR-ABL1 fusion protein is a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Unlike the normal ABL1 protein, which requires specific activation signals, BCR-ABL1 is always "on," leading to uncontrolled phosphorylation of downstream signaling molecules involved in cell proliferation, survival, and differentiation. This drives the excessive production of myeloid cells seen in CML.
- **Imatinib Mechanism:** Imatinib (Gleevec) is a small-molecule tyrosine kinase inhibitor specifically designed to target the ATP-binding site of the BCR-ABL1 kinase domain. By binding to this site, imatinib prevents ATP from binding and thus inhibits the kinase activity of the BCR-ABL1 protein. This effectively blocks the downstream signaling pathways responsible for the malignant phenotype of CML cells.
- **Other TKIs:** Other TKIs like dasatinib, nilotinib, and bosutinib also target BCR-ABL1 but may have different binding affinities, specificities, and resistance profiles.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **JAK2:** Janus kinase 2 (JAK2) is a tyrosine kinase involved in cytokine receptor signaling. Mutations in JAK2 (specifically V617F) are commonly found in other myeloproliferative neoplasms like polycythemia vera, essential thrombocythemia, and primary myelofibrosis, but not typically in CML. Imatinib does not primarily target JAK2.
- (B) **FLT3:** Fms-like tyrosine kinase 3 (FLT3) is a receptor tyrosine kinase involved in hematopoietic cell development. Activating mutations in FLT3 are common in acute myeloid leukemia (AML), particularly AML with normal cytogenetics. Imatinib does not target FLT3.
- (D) **KIT:** KIT is a receptor tyrosine kinase encoded by the KIT proto-oncogene. Activating mutations in KIT are found in gastrointestinal stromal tumors (GISTs), mastocytosis, and some cases of AML and hypereosinophilic syndrome. Imatinib is used to treat GISTs with KIT mutations, but it is not the primary target in CML.
- (E) **ALK:** Anaplastic lymphoma kinase (ALK) is a receptor tyrosine kinase. Rearrangements involving the ALK gene are found in certain types of non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) and anaplastic large cell lymphoma (ALCL). Imatinib does not target ALK. Crizotinib and other ALK inhibitors are used for ALK-positive cancers.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature          | CML (BCR-ABL1)                               | PV/ET/PMF (JAK2)                             | AML (FLT3)                                  | GIST/Mastocytosis (KIT)                   | NSCLC/ALCL (ALK)                             |
|-------------------|---------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| **Primary Disease** | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia                     | Polycythemia Vera, Essential Thrombocythemia, Primary Myelofibrosis | Acute Myeloid Leukemia                      | Gastrointestinal Stromal Tumor, Mastocytosis | Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer, Anaplastic Large Cell Lymphoma |
| **Key Gene**       | BCR-ABL1 (t(9;22))                           | JAK2 (V617F, exon 12)                         | FLT3 (ITD, TKD)                               | KIT (exons 9, 11, 13, 17)                   | ALK (EML4-ALK fusion)                        |
| **Protein Type**   | Constitutively active Tyrosine Kinase       | Constitutively active Tyrosine Kinase        | Constitutively active Tyrosine Kinase        | Constitutively active Tyrosine Kinase        | Constitutively active Tyrosine Kinase        |
| **Targeted Therapy**| Imatinib, Dasatinib, Nilotinib, Bosutinib   | Ruxolitinib, Fedratinib                       | Midostaurin, Gilteritinib, Sorafenib        | Imatinib, Sunitinib                          | Crizotinib, Alectinib, Ceritinib             |
| **Clinical Clues** | Splenomegaly, Leukocytosis, Left Shift, Ph+ | Erythrocytosis, Thrombocytosis, Myelofibrosis | Blasts in bone marrow/blood, Auer rods       | GI tumors, Skin lesions, Mast cell infiltration | Lung nodules, Lymphadenopathy, ALCL histology |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) is defined by the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)) leading to the BCR-ABL1 fusion gene and a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. The first-line treatment, imatinib, specifically targets and inhibits this BCR-ABL1 kinase. Understanding the specific molecular targets of TKIs is crucial for differentiating treatment strategies for various malignancies. Key differentials include other myeloproliferative neoplasms (JAK2 mutations), AML (FLT3 mutations), GIST (KIT mutations), and ALK-positive cancers (ALK rearrangements).

<a id='question-078'></a>

### Question 078: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between primary lymphoid malignancies (lymphoma) and metastatic carcinoma involving lymph nodes based on clinical presentation, morphology, and immunohistochemistry. (区分原发淋巴恶性肿瘤（淋巴瘤）和转移性癌转移到淋巴结，基于临床表现、形态学和免疫组织化学。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a 20-pack-year smoking history presents to the emergency department with a 3-month history of progressive dyspnea, cough, and weight loss (15 lbs). He also reports intermittent right-sided chest pain. His past medical history includes hypertension treated with lisinopril and hyperlipidemia treated with atorvastatin. Physical examination reveals bilateral supraclavicular and axillary lymphadenopathy, which are firm and non-tender. Auscultation reveals decreased breath sounds in the right lower lung field. A chest X-ray shows a large right hilar mass and multiple pulmonary nodules. A CT scan of the chest, abdomen, and pelvis confirms the hilar mass, pulmonary nodules, and widespread lymphadenopathy, including retroperitoneal nodes. Biopsy of a supraclavicular lymph node is performed. Histopathology shows nests of large, atypical epithelial cells with abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, prominent nucleoli, and frequent mitotic figures. These cells form glandular structures in some areas. Immunohistochemical staining is positive for cytokeratins (CK7 and CK20), thyroid transcription factor-1 (TTF-1), and carcinoembryonic antigen (CEA), but negative for CD45, CD20, CD3, and PAX5.

Which of the following is the most likely primary site of malignancy in this patient?

(A) Colon
(B) Breast
(C) Lung
(D) Prostate
(E) Thyroid

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 62-year-old male, smoker (risk factor for lung cancer).
- **Symptoms:** Progressive dyspnea, cough, weight loss, chest pain (suggestive of intrathoracic malignancy).
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender lymphadenopathy (supraclavicular, axillary) - concerning for malignancy, potentially metastatic.
- **Imaging:** Chest X-ray and CT show right hilar mass, pulmonary nodules, widespread lymphadenopathy (suggestive of primary lung cancer with metastasis).
- **Lymph Node Biopsy:**
    - **Histopathology:** Nests of large, atypical epithelial cells with eosinophilic cytoplasm, prominent nucleoli, frequent mitoses, glandular formation (suggestive of adenocarcinoma).
    - **Immunohistochemistry:**
        - Positive for CK7, CK20, TTF-1, CEA (markers typically associated with lung adenocarcinoma, particularly TTF-1).
        - Negative for CD45, CD20, CD3, PAX5 (markers for hematopoietic/lymphoid cells, ruling out primary lymphoma or leukemia).
- **Conclusion:** The clinical presentation (smoker, respiratory symptoms, hilar mass, pulmonary nodules, lymphadenopathy) combined with the histopathology (adenocarcinoma features) and specific immunohistochemical profile (positive TTF-1, CK7, CK20, CEA; negative lymphoid markers) strongly points towards metastatic adenocarcinoma, most likely originating from the lung.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Metastatic Adenocarcinoma:** The biopsy shows features of adenocarcinoma (glandular formation, mucin production often seen, though not explicitly stated here). Adenocarcinoma is a common type of lung cancer.
- **Immunohistochemistry Interpretation:**
    - **CK7 and CK20:** Cytokeratins are intermediate filaments found in epithelial cells. CK7 is often positive in lung, breast, ovary, and mesothelioma. CK20 is often positive in colon, stomach, and urothelium. Co-expression of CK7 and CK20 is common in lung adenocarcinoma.
    - **TTF-1 (Thyroid Transcription Factor-1):** This is a nuclear transcription factor crucial for thyroid development but is also highly specific (though not 100%) for lung adenocarcinoma (especially primary lung adenocarcinoma). It is rarely positive in other carcinomas.
    - **CEA (Carcinoembryonic Antigen):** A cell surface glycoprotein often elevated in various adenocarcinomas, including lung, colon, breast, and pancreas.
    - **CD45 (Leukocyte Common Antigen):** A marker for hematopoietic cells. Negative result rules out lymphoma/leukemia.
    - **CD20, CD3:** Markers for B-cells and T-cells, respectively. Negative results rule out B-cell lymphoma and T-cell lymphoma.
    - **PAX5:** A transcription factor crucial for B-cell development. Negative result rules out B-cell lymphoma.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The patient's smoking history, respiratory symptoms, and imaging findings (hilar mass, pulmonary nodules) are classic for primary lung cancer. The lymph node biopsy confirms metastatic adenocarcinoma with an immunohistochemical profile highly suggestive of lung origin (TTF-1 positive).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Colon: Colon adenocarcinoma typically expresses CK20 but is usually negative for CK7 and TTF-1. While CEA can be positive, the combination of CK7+, CK20+, TTF-1+ is highly atypical for colon cancer. The clinical presentation (respiratory symptoms, hilar mass) is also inconsistent with primary colon cancer.
- (B) Breast: Breast adenocarcinoma can be CK7 positive and sometimes CK20 positive, but it is typically negative for TTF-1 and CEA (though CEA can be positive in some cases). The clinical presentation is not typical for primary breast cancer.
- (D) Prostate: Prostate adenocarcinoma is typically positive for prostate-specific antigen (PSA) and prostatic acid phosphatase (PAP), and negative for CK7, CK20, and TTF-1. The histology (glandular formation) might be superficially similar, but the immunohistochemistry and clinical context are completely wrong.
- (E) Thyroid: Thyroid papillary carcinoma can be positive for TTF-1 and CK7, but it is usually negative for CK20 and CEA. The clinical presentation (respiratory symptoms, hilar mass) is inconsistent with primary thyroid cancer. Follicular carcinoma can also be TTF-1 positive. However, the overall picture strongly favors lung.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Lung Adenocarcinoma | Colon Adenocarcinoma | Breast Adenocarcinoma | Prostate Adenocarcinoma | Thyroid Carcinoma |
|----------------------|----------------------|-----------------------|------------------------|--------------------------|-------------------|
| **Clinical Context** | Smoker, Resp. Sx, Hilar Mass | GI Sx, Colonoscopy | Breast Exam/Mammogram | PSA, GU Sx              | Neck Mass/Thyroid Sx |
| **Histology**        | Glandular, Mucin    | Glandular             | Glandular              | Glandular                | Papillary/Follicular |
| **CK7**              | +                    | -                     | + (variable)           | -                        | +                 |
| **CK20**             | +                    | +                     | - (variable)           | -                        | -                 |
| **TTF-1**            | +                    | -                     | -                      | -                        | -                 |
| **CEA**              | +                    | +                     | +/-                    | -                        | -                 |
| **PSA**              | -                    | -                     | -                      | +                        | -                 |
| **CD45**             | -                    | -                     | -                      | -                        | -                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Lung adenocarcinoma is the most common type of lung cancer and frequently metastasizes to lymph nodes. TTF-1 is a highly specific marker for primary lung adenocarcinoma. Differentiating metastatic carcinoma in lymph nodes from primary lymphoma requires careful histopathological examination and immunohistochemistry, using markers specific for epithelial cells (cytokeratins) versus hematopoietic cells (CD45, CD20, CD3, PAX5). Key differentials for adenocarcinoma in lymph nodes include metastases from colon, breast, prostate, and thyroid, each with distinct immunohistochemical profiles.
