<a id='question-088'></a>

### Question 088: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Hodgkin Lymphoma
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of Reed-Sternberg cells and their characteristic immunohistochemical profile (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-) in the context of Hodgkin lymphoma, differentiating it from other lymphomas and reactive lymphoid proliferations.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of painless swelling in his left supraclavicular lymph node, which he noticed approximately 3 weeks ago. He denies fever, night sweats, or unintentional weight loss ("B symptoms"). His past medical history is unremarkable, and he takes no medications. Physical examination reveals a single, firm, non-tender, rubbery lymph node measuring 2 cm in diameter in the left supraclavicular region. The remainder of the physical examination is normal. An excisional biopsy of the lymph node is performed. Histopathological examination shows a diffuse infiltrate of lymphocytes interspersed with occasional large, atypical cells with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli and bilobed or multilobed nuclei, resembling "owl's eyes". These atypical cells are surrounded by a reactive infiltrate of lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, and histiocytes. Immunohistochemical staining reveals that the large atypical cells are positive for CD30 and CD15, and negative for CD45 and most B-cell markers (CD20, CD79a) and T-cell markers (CD3, CD45RO).

Which of the following cellular markers is most characteristic of the large, atypical cells described in this patient's lymph node biopsy?

(A) CD45 positivity
(B) CD20 positivity
(C) CD3 positivity
(D) CD15 positivity
(E) CD45RO positivity

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 28-year-old male, relatively young adult.
- **Presenting Complaint:** Painless supraclavicular lymphadenopathy (single node).
- **Absence of B symptoms:** No fever, night sweats, or weight loss, which can occur in Hodgkin lymphoma but are not always present, especially in early stages.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender, rubbery lymph node.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse lymphocytic infiltrate with occasional large, atypical cells.
- **Cell Morphology:** Large cells with prominent eosinophilic nucleoli ("owl's eyes") and bilobed/multilobed nuclei. This morphology is highly characteristic of Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells.
- **Reactive Infiltrate:** RS cells are surrounded by reactive lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, and histiocytes. This is a hallmark feature of Hodgkin lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** The key diagnostic information. The large atypical cells (RS cells) are positive for CD30 and CD15, and negative for CD45 (leukocyte common antigen) and B/T cell markers.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Hodgkin Lymphoma (HL):** A malignancy of B-lymphocytes, characterized by the presence of Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells within a reactive inflammatory background.
- **Reed-Sternberg (RS) Cells:** These are the malignant cells in HL. They are derived from germinal center B-cells.
- **Immunophenotype of RS Cells:** RS cells typically lose expression of B-cell markers like CD20 and CD79a, and T-cell markers like CD3 and CD45RO. They characteristically express CD30 and CD15. Loss of CD45 expression is also typical.
- **CD Markers:**
    - **CD30:** A member of the tumor necrosis factor receptor superfamily, often upregulated in HL and anaplastic large cell lymphoma.
    - **CD15:** A carbohydrate antigen (Lewis X antigen), also commonly expressed in HL.
    - **CD45:** The leukocyte common antigen, expressed on most hematopoietic cells except mature erythrocytes and platelets. RS cells typically lack CD45 expression.
    - **CD20:** A B-cell marker, typically lost in classical HL RS cells.
    - **CD3:** A T-cell marker, negative in HL.
    - **CD45RO:** An isoform of CD45 expressed on memory T-cells, negative in HL.
- **Diagnosis:** The combination of characteristic RS cell morphology and immunophenotype (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-) in the context of a reactive inflammatory infiltrate confirms the diagnosis of classical Hodgkin lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **CD45 positivity:** Incorrect. RS cells in Hodgkin lymphoma typically *lack* CD45 expression. CD45 is a pan-leukocyte marker, and its absence helps distinguish RS cells from other lymphoma cells and reactive lymphocytes.
- (B) **CD20 positivity:** Incorrect. While RS cells are derived from B-cells, they typically *lose* expression of most B-cell markers, including CD20, in classical Hodgkin lymphoma. CD20 positivity would suggest a B-cell lymphoma other than classical HL (e.g., diffuse large B-cell lymphoma) or potentially nodular lymphocyte-predominant Hodgkin lymphoma (NLPHL), which has a different immunophenotype (CD20+, CD15-, CD30-).
- (C) **CD3 positivity:** Incorrect. CD3 is a T-cell marker. RS cells are of B-cell origin and are negative for T-cell markers like CD3.
- (D) **CD15 positivity:** Correct. CD15 is a characteristic marker expressed by the Reed-Sternberg cells in classical Hodgkin lymphoma, along with CD30.
- (E) **CD45RO positivity:** Incorrect. CD45RO is a marker for memory T-cells. RS cells are of B-cell origin and are negative for T-cell markers, including CD45RO.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Hodgkin Lymphoma (Classical) | Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) | T-cell Lymphoma | Reactive Lymphadenitis |
|----------------------|------------------------------|---------------------------------------|-----------------|------------------------|
| **Key Cell**         | Reed-Sternberg (RS) Cell     | Large atypical B-cell                 | Atypical T-cell | Reactive lymphocytes  |
| **RS Cell Morphology**| Owl's eye, bilobed/multilobed | Variable, often large and cleaved      | Variable        | Normal                 |
| **Background**       | Reactive infiltrate (lymphocytes, eosinophils, plasma cells, histiocytes) | Diffuse sheets of atypical cells | Sheets of atypical cells | Polyclonal lymphocytes |
| **CD30**             | Positive (+)                 | Variable (often negative or weak +)    | Negative (-)    | Negative (-)           |
| **CD15**             | Positive (+)                 | Negative (-)                          | Negative (-)    | Negative (-)           |
| **CD45**             | Negative (-)                 | Positive (+)                          | Positive (+)    | Positive (+)           |
| **CD20**             | Negative (-)                 | Positive (+)                          | Negative (-)    | Negative (-)           |
| **CD3**              | Negative (-)                 | Negative (-)                          | Positive (+)    | Negative (-)           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Hodgkin lymphoma is characterized by malignant Reed-Sternberg cells (CD30+, CD15+, CD45-) within a reactive inflammatory background. Recognizing the unique immunophenotype of RS cells is crucial for diagnosis. Differentiating HL from other lymphomas (like DLBCL or T-cell lymphomas) relies heavily on morphology and immunohistochemistry.

<a id='question-089'></a>

### Question 089: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-39 (• Histiocytic and dendritic cell neoplasms)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of Langerhans cell histiocytosis (LCH), specifically its pathophysiology involving clonal proliferation of Langerhans cells, the characteristic immunohistochemical markers (CD1a, S100, Birbeck granules), and the associated genetic mutations (BRAF V600E).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 3-year-old boy presents to the emergency department with a 3-month history of worsening irritability, low-grade fever, and a persistent, non-productive cough. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he is not on any medications. Physical examination reveals hepatosplenomegaly, generalized lymphadenopathy, and multiple punched-out lytic bone lesions on skeletal survey. Complete blood count (CBC) shows mild anemia (Hb 9.5 g/dL, reference range 11-14 g/dL), thrombocytopenia (platelets 80,000/µL, reference range 150,000-450,000/µL), and leukocytosis with atypical lymphocytes (WBC 18,000/µL, reference range 4,500-11,000/µL). A bone marrow biopsy is performed. Histopathology shows infiltration of the marrow by large cells with abundant eosinophilic cytoplasm, vesicular nuclei with prominent nucleoli, and admixed lymphocytes, plasma cells, and eosinophils. Immunohistochemical staining reveals that the large cells are positive for CD1a, S100, and Langerin (CD207), and electron microscopy shows Birbeck granules. Which of the following molecular alterations is most commonly associated with this patient's condition?

(A) Philadelphia chromosome translocation t(9;22)
(B) MYC amplification
(C) BRAF V600E mutation
(D) NPM1 mutation
(E) FLT3 internal tandem duplication (ITD)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 3-year-old boy - LCH typically affects children and young adults.
- **Symptoms:** Irritability, low-grade fever, persistent cough (3 months) - Suggests a chronic, systemic process. Cough could indicate lung involvement.
- **Physical Exam:** Hepatosplenomegaly, generalized lymphadenopathy, punched-out lytic bone lesions - Classic triad of LCH (diabetes insipidus, bone lesions, skin rash), although this patient presents with systemic involvement (liver, spleen, lymph nodes, bone). Bone lesions are highly characteristic.
- **Labs:** Mild anemia, thrombocytopenia, leukocytosis with atypical lymphocytes - Suggests bone marrow involvement and potential infiltration by abnormal cells.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Infiltration by large cells with eosinophilic cytoplasm, vesicular nuclei, prominent nucleoli, admixed lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils - Describes the characteristic histopathology of LCH.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** Positive for CD1a, S100, Langerin (CD207) - These are the hallmark immunohistochemical markers for Langerhans cells, confirming the diagnosis of LCH.
- **Electron Microscopy:** Birbeck granules - Pathognomonic ultrastructural feature of Langerhans cells, further confirming the diagnosis.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most common molecular alteration associated with this condition (LCH).

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis (LCH):** A clonal neoplastic disorder characterized by the proliferation and accumulation of abnormal Langerhans cells (LCs), which are dendritic cells normally found in the epidermis and other tissues.
- **Pathophysiology:** The clonal proliferation of LCs leads to tissue infiltration and destruction, causing the diverse clinical manifestations seen in LCH. The exact trigger for this clonal expansion is not fully understood, but it involves genetic alterations affecting LC signaling pathways.
- **Molecular Genetics:** The most common genetic alteration found in LCH is a somatic activating mutation in the *BRAF* gene, specifically the V600E mutation (approximately 50-60% of cases). This mutation leads to constitutive activation of the BRAF kinase, which is part of the MAPK signaling pathway, promoting cell proliferation and survival. Other mutations in the MAPK pathway (e.g., *MAP2K1*) can also occur.
- **Diagnosis:** Based on clinical presentation, histopathology (LC infiltration), and immunohistochemistry (CD1a+, S100+, CD207+). Electron microscopy showing Birbeck granules is confirmatory but not always necessary.
- **Treatment:** Varies depending on the extent and severity of disease. BRAF inhibitors (e.g., vemurafenib, dabrafenib) and MEK inhibitors (e.g., trametinib, cobimetinib) have shown efficacy in refractory or multisystem LCH, targeting the activated MAPK pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Philadelphia chromosome translocation t(9;22): This translocation creates the BCR-ABL fusion gene, which is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and some cases of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). It is not associated with LCH.
- (B) MYC amplification: Amplification of the *MYC* oncogene is commonly found in Burkitt lymphoma, a high-grade B-cell lymphoma. It is not associated with LCH.
- (D) NPM1 mutation: Mutations in the *NPM1* gene are frequently found in Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML), particularly AML with normal cytogenetics. They are not associated with LCH.
- (E) FLT3 internal tandem duplication (ITD): *FLT3*-ITD mutations are common in AML, associated with a poorer prognosis. They are not associated with LCH.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Langerhans Cell Histiocytosis (LCH) | Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) | Burkitt Lymphoma | Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML) |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------|--------------------------------|-------------------|------------------------------|
| **Cell Type**        | Langerhans Cells (Dendritic Cells)   | Myeloid Progenitors            | B-lymphocytes     | Myeloid Progenitors          |
| **Age**              | Primarily Children/Young Adults      | Adults (Median ~65)            | Children/Young Adults | Adults (Median ~67)          |
| **Key Clinical Features** | Bone lesions, DI, skin rash, systemic | Splenomegaly, leukocytosis, fatigue | Jaw mass, abdominal mass | Fatigue, infection, bleeding |
| **Histology**        | LC infiltration, admixed cells       | Hypercellular marrow, myeloid hyperplasia | Diffuse lymphoma cells | Blasts in marrow/blood       |
| **Immunohistochemistry** | CD1a+, S100+, CD207+, Birbeck granules | CD34+, CD38+, CD117+           | CD20+, CD10+, MYC+   | CD34+, CD38+, MPO+ (AML)     |
| **Genetic Alteration** | BRAF V600E                         | t(9;22) (BCR-ABL)              | MYC amplification | NPM1 mutation, FLT3-ITD      |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Langerhans cell histiocytosis (LCH) is a clonal proliferation of Langerhans cells, identifiable by their characteristic immunohistochemical profile (CD1a+, S100+, CD207+) and ultrastructural feature (Birbeck granules). The most common underlying molecular alteration is a BRAF V600E mutation. Remember to differentiate LCH from other pediatric malignancies like leukemia/lymphoma and other histiocytic disorders based on morphology, immunophenotype, and clinical presentation.

<a id='question-090'></a>

### Question 090: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Posttransplantation Lymphoproliferative Disorder (PTLD)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of Posttransplantation Lymphoproliferative Disorder (PTLD), specifically its association with Epstein-Barr Virus (EBV) infection in the context of immunosuppression following organ transplantation, and the resulting B-cell lymphoproliferation. (考察移植后淋巴增生性疾病 (PTLD)，其与器官移植后免疫抑制背景下EB病毒 (EBV) 感染的关系，以及由此导致的B细胞淋巴增殖。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 52-year-old male with a history of end-stage renal disease secondary to diabetic nephropathy received a kidney transplant 18 months ago. He is currently maintained on immunosuppressive therapy consisting of tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, and prednisone. Over the past 3 months, he has experienced progressive fatigue, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss of 10 lbs. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, including cervical, axillary, and inguinal nodes, which are firm and non-tender. A computed tomography (CT) scan of the chest, abdomen, and pelvis shows widespread lymphadenopathy and splenomegaly. A biopsy of a cervical lymph node reveals a diffuse proliferation of atypical large B lymphocytes with prominent nucleoli and high mitotic activity. Immunohistochemical staining shows the atypical cells are positive for CD20, CD30, and LMP-1 (Latent Membrane Protein 1), and negative for CD15. EBV in situ hybridization is strongly positive within the atypical lymphocytes. His serum creatinine is 1.8 mg/dL (reference range: 0.6-1.2 mg/dL), indicating graft function is stable. Which of the following is the most likely underlying mechanism driving the development of this patient's condition?

(A) Somatic hypermutation leading to uncontrolled proliferation of B cells in the germinal center, independent of EBV infection.
(B) Reactivation of latent EBV infection due to immunosuppression, leading to immortalization and proliferation of recipient B cells expressing LMP-1.
(C) T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against donor-specific antigens, resulting in chronic rejection and subsequent B-cell activation.
(D) Direct oncogenic transformation of donor T cells by immunosuppressive drugs, leading to a T-cell lymphoma.
(E) Autoantibody production against B-cell receptors, causing chronic B-cell stimulation and lymphoproliferation.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
*   **Patient Profile:** 52-year-old male, kidney transplant recipient (18 months ago), on immunosuppression (tacrolimus, mycophenolate mofetil, prednisone). This sets the stage for post-transplant complications, particularly those related to immunosuppression.
*   **Symptoms:** Progressive fatigue, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms) over 3 months. These are constitutional symptoms often associated with lymphoma.
*   **Physical Exam:** Generalized lymphadenopathy (firm, non-tender). This is a hallmark sign of lymphoma.
*   **Imaging:** CT scan confirms widespread lymphadenopathy and splenomegaly, further supporting a systemic lymphoproliferative process.
*   **Lymph Node Biopsy:** Diffuse proliferation of atypical large B lymphocytes (morphology suggestive of lymphoma), high mitotic activity.
*   **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD30+ (often seen in Hodgkin lymphoma and some aggressive B-cell lymphomas), LMP-1+ (EBV-encoded protein), CD15- (typically negative in most B-cell lymphomas, positive in classical Hodgkin lymphoma).
*   **EBV In Situ Hybridization:** Strongly positive within atypical lymphocytes. This is a crucial finding linking the proliferation to EBV.
*   **Graft Function:** Stable serum creatinine indicates the kidney transplant is functioning well, making acute rejection less likely as the primary cause of the lymphoproliferation.
*   **Question Stem:** Asks for the *underlying mechanism*.

The combination of recent organ transplant, immunosuppression, B symptoms, generalized lymphadenopathy, biopsy showing atypical large B cells, positive CD20, CD30, LMP-1, and strong EBV positivity points strongly towards Posttransplantation Lymphoproliferative Disorder (PTLD), specifically an EBV-driven B-cell lymphoma.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
*   **PTLD Pathogenesis:** PTLD is a spectrum of lymphoid proliferations occurring after solid organ or hematopoietic stem cell transplantation. It is strongly associated with immunosuppression, which impairs T-cell surveillance against EBV-infected B cells.
*   **EBV Role:** EBV is a ubiquitous herpesvirus. In immunocompetent individuals, primary EBV infection is usually controlled by cytotoxic T lymphocytes (CTLs). However, in transplant recipients receiving immunosuppressive drugs (especially T-cell inhibitors like tacrolimus and mycophenolate mofetil), this CTL response is blunted.
*   **Mechanism:**
    1.  **Reactivation/Primary Infection:** EBV can reactivate latently or cause primary infection in the transplant recipient.
    2.  **B-cell Infection:** EBV infects B lymphocytes.
    3.  **Impaired T-cell Control:** Immunosuppression prevents effective CTL killing of EBV-infected B cells.
    4.  **B-cell Immortalization:** EBV viral proteins, particularly LMP-1 (Latent Membrane Protein 1), mimic CD40 signaling. CD40 is a crucial co-stimulatory molecule for B-cell activation and survival, normally engaged by CD40L on activated T cells. LMP-1 constitutively activates downstream signaling pathways (NF-κB, MAPK, PI3K/Akt), promoting B-cell proliferation, survival (anti-apoptosis), and differentiation, effectively bypassing the need for T-cell help.
    5.  **Lymphoproliferation:** The immortalized B cells proliferate uncontrollably, leading to PTLD.
*   **Histology/Immunophenotype:** PTLD often presents as a diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), characterized by large atypical B cells (CD20+), often with CD30 expression. The presence of LMP-1 and EBV positivity confirms the EBV-driven nature.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Somatic hypermutation leading to uncontrolled proliferation of B cells in the germinal center, independent of EBV infection.** While somatic hypermutation occurs in germinal centers and can lead to B-cell lymphoma (e.g., follicular lymphoma), this option incorrectly states it's independent of EBV. The vignette strongly implicates EBV (positive in situ hybridization, LMP-1 expression). Furthermore, PTLD is specifically linked to immunosuppression and EBV, not just general somatic hypermutation.
- (C): **T-cell mediated cytotoxicity against donor-specific antigens, resulting in chronic rejection and subsequent B-cell activation.** This describes chronic rejection, which typically involves graft dysfunction (e.g., rising creatinine) and fibrosis, not widespread lymphoproliferation driven by EBV. While chronic rejection can occur, it doesn't explain the specific histological and molecular findings (EBV+, LMP-1+, atypical B cells). The patient's stable graft function also argues against this being the primary mechanism.
- (D): **Direct oncogenic transformation of donor T cells by immunosuppressive drugs, leading to a T-cell lymphoma.** PTLD is primarily a B-cell proliferation driven by EBV in the recipient. While immunosuppression can increase the risk of other malignancies, including T-cell lymphomas (e.g., cutaneous T-cell lymphoma), the biopsy findings (CD20+ B cells, LMP-1+) clearly point to a B-cell origin, not T-cell.
- (E): **Autoantibody production against B-cell receptors, causing chronic B-cell stimulation and lymphoproliferation.** This mechanism is not typically associated with PTLD. While autoantibodies can play a role in some autoimmune conditions or lymphomas, the hallmark of PTLD is EBV-driven immortalization of B cells due to impaired T-cell control under immunosuppression. The presence of LMP-1 and EBV positivity strongly contradicts this mechanism.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature                 | PTLD (Correct Answer)                                  | Somatic Hypermutation (Distractor A)                 | Chronic Rejection (Distractor C)                     | T-cell Lymphoma (Distractor D)                       | Autoantibody Stimulation (Distractor E)              |
| :---------------------- | :----------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------- | :---------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Cell Type**   | B-lymphocytes                                          | B-lymphocytes                                        | N/A (Graft damage)                                    | T-lymphocytes                                        | B-lymphocytes                                        |
| **Driving Force**       | EBV infection + Immunosuppression                       | Somatic hypermutation errors                          | T-cell attack on donor antigens                       | Immunosuppression (indirectly)                      | Autoantibodies                                       |
| **Key Molecular Marker** | LMP-1, EBV positivity                                  | N/A                                                  | N/A                                                   | N/A                                                  | N/A                                                  |
| **Clinical Context**    | Post-transplant, immunosuppressed                       | Can occur in immunocompetent or immunocompromised   | Post-transplant, graft dysfunction                   | Post-transplant, immunosuppressed                       | Various autoimmune/lymphoproliferative conditions    |
| **Histology**           | Atypical large B cells, diffuse pattern often          | Variable, often follicular pattern                    | Graft fibrosis, inflammation                           | Atypical T cells                                      | Variable B-cell proliferation                       |
| **Immunophenotype**     | CD20+, often CD30+, LMP-1+                               | CD10+, BCL2+, CD20+ (e.g., Follicular Lymphoma)       | N/A                                                   | CD3+, CD4/CD8+                                        | CD20+                                                |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
PTLD is a critical complication in transplant recipients, driven by EBV reactivation/infection in the setting of immunosuppression. The key mechanism involves EBV-mediated immortalization of B cells via viral proteins like LMP-1, bypassing normal T-cell control. Remember to differentiate PTLD from other post-transplant complications like acute/chronic rejection or other malignancies. Key diagnostic features include lymphoproliferation, B-cell phenotype (CD20+), and evidence of EBV infection (in situ hybridization, LMP-1 expression).

<a id='question-091'></a>

### Question 091: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-41 (Mature B Cell Neoplasms)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiate between various mature B-cell neoplasms based on clinical presentation, morphology, immunophenotype, and genetic features, specifically focusing on the characteristic translocation and resulting protein in Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18) leading to BCL2 overexpression).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old woman presents to her primary care physician with a 6-month history of progressive fatigue, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss (10 lbs). Her past medical history is significant for hypertension treated with lisinopril and hyperlipidemia treated with atorvastatin. She denies fever, chills, or recent infections. Physical examination reveals generalized, painless lymphadenopathy, particularly prominent in the cervical and axillary regions. The spleen is palpable 3 cm below the left costal margin. Complete blood count (CBC) shows mild normocytic anemia (Hgb 11.5 g/dL, reference range 12-16 g/dL), normal white blood cell count (WBC 6,500/µL, reference range 4,000-11,000/µL) with a normal differential, and normal platelet count. A lymph node biopsy from the cervical region is performed. Histopathology shows effacement of the normal lymph node architecture by a diffuse proliferation of small to medium-sized lymphoid cells with cleaved nuclei and pale cytoplasm. The cells grow in a nodular pattern separated by fibrous septa, creating lymphoid follicles. Immunohistochemistry reveals the neoplastic cells are positive for CD19, CD20, CD10, and BCL6, and negative for CD5 and cyclin D1. Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) analysis demonstrates a translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14 and the BCL2 locus on chromosome 18, t(14;18)(q32;q21). Which of the following molecular events is most directly responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to translocation t(8;14).
(B) Overexpression of the BCL2 protein due to translocation t(14;18).
(C) Deficiency of the CD5 protein leading to impaired T-cell signaling.
(D) Overexpression of the cyclin D1 protein due to translocation t(11;14).
(E) Deficiency of the BCL6 protein leading to germinal center B-cell apoptosis.

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 62-year-old woman with progressive fatigue, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), generalized painless lymphadenopathy, and splenomegaly. This constellation suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder, likely lymphoma.
- **CBC:** Mild anemia, normal WBC and platelets. This is common in indolent lymphomas like follicular lymphoma, where bone marrow involvement is often minimal initially.
- **Lymph Node Biopsy:** Histopathology shows effacement of architecture, nodular pattern with lymphoid follicles, small to medium-sized cells with cleaved nuclei. This morphology is highly characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, CD5-, cyclin D1-. This immunophenotype is classic for Follicular Lymphoma (germinal center B-cell origin). CD5 negativity helps exclude Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia/Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (CLL/SLL) and Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Cyclin D1 negativity excludes Mantle Cell Lymphoma.
- **FISH:** t(14;18)(q32;q21). This is the hallmark genetic abnormality of Follicular Lymphoma.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular event responsible for the pathogenesis.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- The patient's clinical presentation, histology (nodular pattern, cleaved cells), and immunophenotype (CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, CD5-, cyclin D1-) strongly point towards Follicular Lymphoma.
- The FISH result confirms the diagnosis by identifying the characteristic t(14;18) translocation.
- This translocation juxtaposes the BCL2 gene (on chromosome 18) next to the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) gene locus (on chromosome 14).
- The IGH locus is highly active in B-cells, leading to constitutive overexpression of the BCL2 protein.
- BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein. Its overexpression inhibits programmed cell death (apoptosis) in the germinal center B-cells, allowing them to survive longer than normal and accumulate, leading to the development of follicular lymphoma.
- Therefore, the overexpression of BCL2 protein due to the t(14;18) translocation is the direct molecular event responsible for the pathogenesis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Overexpression of the MYC oncogene due to translocation t(8;14). This translocation is characteristic of Burkitt Lymphoma, which typically presents as a rapidly growing mass, often extranodal, and involves cells with a "starry sky" appearance histologically. The immunophenotype is usually CD10+, BCL6+, but often lacks CD19/CD20 or has atypical expression. This does not fit the patient's presentation or findings.
- (C) Deficiency of the CD5 protein leading to impaired T-cell signaling. CD5 is primarily expressed on T-cells and a subset of B-cells (like in CLL/SLL and Mantle Cell Lymphoma). Deficiency of CD5 is not a pathogenic mechanism in any common lymphoma. Furthermore, the patient's cells are CD5 negative. This option is incorrect on multiple levels.
- (D) Overexpression of the cyclin D1 protein due to translocation t(11;14). This translocation is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Mantle Cell Lymphoma typically involves small to medium-sized lymphocytes with irregular nuclei, often expresses CD5, and is cyclin D1 positive. The patient's cells are cyclin D1 negative. This does not fit the patient's presentation or findings.
- (E) Deficiency of the BCL6 protein leading to germinal center B-cell apoptosis. BCL6 is a transcription factor crucial for germinal center formation and B-cell survival within the germinal center. While BCL6 mutations or loss can occur in certain lymphomas (like Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma, DLBCL), its deficiency is not the primary pathogenic mechanism in Follicular Lymphoma. In fact, Follicular Lymphoma cells typically *express* BCL6. The pathogenesis involves *inhibition* of apoptosis, not promotion of it via BCL6 deficiency.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Follicular Lymphoma (Correct Answer) | Burkitt Lymphoma | Mantle Cell Lymphoma | CLL/SLL |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------|-------------------|----------------------|---------|
| **Genetics**         | t(14;18) BCL2 overexpression         | t(8;14) MYC        | t(11;14) Cyclin D1   | del(13q), trisomy 12, del(11q) |
| **Morphology**       | Nodular, cleaved cells               | Diffuse, starry sky | Diffuse/nodular, irregular nuclei | Small lymphocytes, smudge cells |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD19+, CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, CD5-, cyclin D1- | CD10+, BCL6+       | CD19+, CD20+, CD5+, cyclin D1+ | CD19+, CD20+, CD5+, CD23+ |
| **Clinical Course**  | Indolent                             | Aggressive        | Intermediate/Aggressive | Indolent |
| **Key Pathogenesis** | BCL2 overexpression (anti-apoptotic) | MYC overexpression (proliferation) | Cyclin D1 overexpression (cell cycle) | B-cell receptor signaling, anti-apoptotic defects |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Follicular Lymphoma is characterized by the t(14;18) translocation leading to BCL2 overexpression and inhibition of apoptosis in germinal center B-cells. Key differentials include other mature B-cell lymphomas like Burkitt (t(8;14), MYC), Mantle Cell (t(11;14), Cyclin D1), and CLL/SLL (CD5+). Understanding the specific genetic translocations and their resulting protein products is crucial for diagnosis and targeted therapy.

<a id='question-092'></a>

### Question 092: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-42 (Chronic lymphocytic leukemia (CLL) and small lymph)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL), specifically the role of BCL-2 anti-apoptotic protein and its regulation by microRNAs, leading to clonal B-cell accumulation. (理解慢性淋巴细胞白血病 (CLL) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCL-2 抗凋亡蛋白的作用及其受微 RNA 的调控，导致克隆 B 细胞积累。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia presents to his primary care physician for a routine check-up. He reports feeling unusually fatigued over the past few months and has noticed some painless swelling in his neck and armpits. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions, and mild splenomegaly. His complete blood count (CBC) shows a markedly elevated white blood cell count (WBC) of 185 x 10^9/L (normal range: 4-11 x 10^9/L) with a predominance of small, mature-appearing lymphocytes. Flow cytometry analysis of the peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals clonal B-cell proliferation expressing CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23. Cytogenetic analysis shows a normal karyotype. A bone marrow biopsy confirms infiltration by the same clonal B-cell population. The patient is diagnosed with Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL). He is initially managed conservatively. However, 18 months later, he presents with worsening fatigue, significant weight loss, night sweats, and rapidly enlarging lymph nodes. Repeat CBC shows a WBC count of 350 x 10^9/L, and a CT scan reveals widespread lymphadenopathy and hepatosplenomegaly. Flow cytometry confirms the persistence of the clonal B-cell population, now expressing significantly lower levels of CD20. Which of the following molecular mechanisms is most likely responsible for the progression of this patient's CLL from an indolent course to a more aggressive phase?

(A) Increased expression of pro-apoptotic proteins like BAX and BAK due to somatic mutations.
(B) Downregulation of the PI3K/AKT signaling pathway, leading to decreased cell survival.
(C) Upregulation of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL-2 due to deletion or mutation of microRNA-15a/16-1.
(D) Activation of the NF-κB pathway, promoting inflammation but not directly influencing cell survival in CLL cells.
(E) Increased expression of Fas ligand (FasL) on CLL cells, leading to enhanced apoptosis via interaction with Fas receptor on T cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 68-year-old male with fatigue, painless lymphadenopathy, splenomegaly. This suggests a lymphoproliferative disorder.
- **Initial Diagnosis:** CBC shows high WBC with mature lymphocytes. Flow cytometry confirms clonal B-cells expressing CD5, CD19, CD20(dim), CD23. This is classic for CLL. Normal karyotype is common in early CLL.
- **Progression:** After 18 months, the patient experiences clinical deterioration (fatigue, weight loss, night sweats, rapidly enlarging nodes, worsening splenomegaly, higher WBC). This indicates disease progression, often referred to as Richter's transformation or progression to a more aggressive phase.
- **Flow Cytometry Change:** Significantly lower CD20 expression is noted. This is a known phenomenon in progressing CLL, often associated with resistance to rituximab (an anti-CD20 antibody).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular mechanism responsible for the progression to a more aggressive phase.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CLL Pathogenesis:** CLL is characterized by the accumulation of monoclonal, mature-appearing B lymphocytes. A key factor in the survival of CLL cells is the overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL-2.
- **BCL-2 Regulation:** BCL-2 is normally regulated by microRNAs, specifically microRNA-15a (miR-15a) and microRNA-16-1 (miR-16-1). These microRNAs target the 3' untranslated region (3'UTR) of the BCL-2 mRNA, leading to its degradation and thus inhibiting BCL-2 protein production.
- **Genetic Alterations in CLL:** Deletions or mutations involving the chromosomal region 13q14, where the miR-15a/miR-16-1 cluster is located, are common genetic events in CLL. These deletions lead to reduced levels of miR-15a and miR-16-1.
- **Consequence of Reduced MicroRNAs:** The reduction in miR-15a/miR-16-1 results in decreased degradation of BCL-2 mRNA, leading to increased BCL-2 protein levels. BCL-2 inhibits apoptosis by binding to and sequestering pro-apoptotic proteins like BAX and BAK, preventing mitochondrial outer membrane permeabilization and caspase activation.
- **Progression and BCL-2:** The progression of CLL to a more aggressive phase is often associated with further genetic alterations, including mutations in genes involved in cell survival and proliferation pathways (e.g., TP53, NOTCH1, SF3B1). However, the fundamental mechanism underlying the survival advantage of CLL cells, which contributes to their accumulation and progression, remains the overexpression of BCL-2 due to the loss of microRNA regulation. The decreased CD20 expression observed in the progressing phase is also a significant clinical feature, often linked to resistance to anti-CD20 therapies. The question specifically asks for the molecular mechanism driving the *progression* related to survival, pointing towards the BCL-2 pathway.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Increased expression of pro-apoptotic proteins like BAX and BAK would *promote* apoptosis, which is contrary to the survival advantage seen in CLL cells and their progression. CLL cells survive *despite* apoptotic signals due to BCL-2 overexpression, which inhibits BAX/BAK. This option describes the opposite effect.
- (B): Downregulation of the PI3K/AKT pathway would *decrease* cell survival and proliferation. The PI3K/AKT pathway is often *activated* in CLL and contributes to cell survival, often downstream of B-cell receptor signaling. Downregulation would hinder progression.
- (D): Activation of the NF-κB pathway is involved in inflammation and can promote cell survival in some cancers, but it is not the primary or most characteristic molecular mechanism driving the survival and accumulation of CLL cells. While NF-κB can be activated in CLL, the BCL-2 pathway is more central to its pathogenesis and progression.
- (E): Increased expression of FasL on CLL cells would lead to apoptosis of Fas-expressing cells (like T cells), potentially contributing to immunosuppression seen in CLL. However, CLL cells themselves are often resistant to Fas-mediated apoptosis due to various mechanisms, including BCL-2 overexpression. Increased FasL does not directly explain the survival and accumulation of the CLL clone itself.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | CLL Pathogenesis (Progression)                                  | Option A (Pro-apoptotic ↑)             | Option B (PI3K/AKT ↓)                | Option D (NF-κB ↑)                     | Option E (FasL ↑)                       |
|----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|--------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|
| **Key Mechanism**    | BCL-2 overexpression inhibits apoptosis                        | Promotes apoptosis                     | Inhibits survival/proliferation      | Primarily inflammation, some survival | Induces apoptosis in Fas+ cells        |
| **Molecular Driver** | Loss of miR-15a/16-1 → ↑ BCL-2 mRNA/protein                     | N/A                                   | N/A                                  | N/A                                   | N/A                                   |
| **Effect on CLL Cells**| Increased survival, clonal expansion, resistance to apoptosis | Decreased survival, apoptosis          | Decreased survival, proliferation    | Variable, not primary survival driver | Does not directly affect CLL survival |
| **Relevance to Progression**| Central to survival advantage, contributes to accumulation | Contradicts CLL survival mechanism    | Contradicts CLL survival mechanism  | Less central than BCL-2               | Not primary mechanism for CLL survival |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL) progression is often driven by mechanisms that enhance the survival of the malignant B cells. A key factor is the overexpression of the anti-apoptotic protein BCL-2, frequently resulting from deletions or mutations affecting the microRNA cluster miR-15a/16-1. This leads to decreased BCL-2 mRNA degradation and increased protein levels, inhibiting apoptosis and promoting clonal expansion. Understanding this pathway is crucial for targeted therapies like venetoclax, a BCL-2 inhibitor. Differential diagnoses for lymphoproliferative disorders include other leukemias (e.g., ALL, AML) and lymphomas (e.g., follicular lymphoma, mantle cell lymphoma), each with distinct genetic and molecular drivers.

<a id='question-093'></a>

### Question 093: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-43
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiating between Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (SLL) and Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL) based on the primary site of involvement and clinical presentation, understanding the underlying B-cell neoplasm and its characteristic immunophenotype. (区分小淋巴细胞淋巴瘤 (SLL) 和慢性淋巴细胞白血病 (CLL) 的依据是主要累及部位和临床表现，理解其潜在的 B 细胞肿瘤及其特征性免疫表型。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male presents to his primary care physician for a routine check-up. He reports feeling unusually fatigued over the past few months and has noticed some swollen lymph nodes in his neck and armpits. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions, with nodes measuring up to 3 cm in diameter, non-tender, and rubbery. Splenomegaly is also noted on abdominal palpation. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a white blood cell (WBC) count of 25,000/µL (reference range: 4,000-11,000/µL) with a differential showing a marked lymphocytosis (90% lymphocytes). Peripheral blood smear reveals numerous small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. Flow cytometry on peripheral blood lymphocytes shows positivity for CD5, CD19, CD20 (dim), and CD23, and negativity for CD10 and FMC7. Bone marrow biopsy reveals infiltration by small lymphocytes, accounting for 30% of the marrow cellularity. Cytogenetic analysis shows del(13q).

Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

(A) Follicular Lymphoma
(B) Mantle Cell Lymphoma
(C) Hairy Cell Leukemia
(D) Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (SLL)
(E) Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL)

#### Correct Answer: (E)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 68-year-old male, age group typical for CLL/SLL.
- **Symptoms:** Fatigue, generalized lymphadenopathy (cervical, axillary), splenomegaly. These are common symptoms of indolent B-cell lymphomas/leukemias.
- **CBC:** Elevated WBC count (25,000/µL) with marked lymphocytosis (90%). This indicates a leukemic process involving lymphocytes.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Small, mature-appearing lymphocytes with clumped chromatin and scant cytoplasm. This morphology is characteristic of CLL/SLL cells.
- **Flow Cytometry:** CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+, CD10-, FMC7-. This immunophenotype is classic for CLL/SLL. CD5 is typically expressed on T cells but aberrantly on CLL/SLL B cells. CD23 positivity is also characteristic. Dim CD20 and negativity for CD10 and FMC7 help distinguish it from other B-cell lymphomas like follicular lymphoma.
- **Bone Marrow Biopsy:** Infiltration by small lymphocytes (30%). This confirms bone marrow involvement, a key feature of leukemia.
- **Cytogenetics:** del(13q). This is a common cytogenetic abnormality in CLL/SLL, often associated with a more indolent course.
- **Distinction between SLL and CLL:** The key difference lies in the primary site of involvement. CLL is defined by the presence of ≥5,000/µL clonal B lymphocytes in the peripheral blood, while SLL is characterized by lymphadenopathy and/or splenomegaly with <5,000/µL clonal B lymphocytes in the peripheral blood. In this case, the patient has both significant lymphadenopathy/splenomegaly *and* a high peripheral blood lymphocyte count (25,000/µL). When both significant peripheral blood involvement (leukemia) and nodal/tissue involvement (lymphoma) are present, the diagnosis is typically Chronic Lymphocytic Leukemia (CLL). SLL is essentially the tissue-based manifestation of the same underlying disease process as CLL.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Pathophysiology:** CLL/SLL is a clonal B-cell neoplasm characterized by the accumulation of mature but functionally incompetent lymphocytes. These cells are resistant to apoptosis and accumulate in the blood, bone marrow, lymph nodes, and spleen.
- **Molecular Mechanisms:** The disease arises from genetic mutations and epigenetic alterations in B lymphocytes. Common genetic abnormalities include deletions of chromosome 13q (as seen in this case), 11q, 17p (TP53 mutation), and trisomy 12. Mutations in genes involved in B-cell receptor signaling (e.g., IGLL1, IGKV) and apoptosis regulation (e.g., TP53, BCL2) are also frequent. The accumulation of these cells leads to immune dysfunction (hypogammaglobulinemia), autoimmune phenomena, and cytopenias due to bone marrow infiltration.
- **Immunophenotype:** The characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+) is crucial for diagnosis and helps distinguish CLL/SLL from other B-cell lymphomas. CD5 expression on B cells is normally restricted to a small subset of B-1 cells but is aberrantly expressed in CLL/SLL. CD23 is a low-affinity IgE receptor often positive in CLL/SLL. Dim CD20 expression is also typical.
- **Clinical Course:** CLL/SLL typically follows an indolent course, but some patients may progress to more aggressive phases or require treatment due to symptomatic disease or complications.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) **Follicular Lymphoma:** This is a common indolent B-cell lymphoma typically presenting with generalized lymphadenopathy. However, it usually does *not* involve the peripheral blood to the extent seen here (leukemic phase is less common). The immunophenotype is typically CD10+, BCL2+, BCL6+, which differs significantly from the patient's profile (CD10-). Histologically, it shows follicular growth patterns.
- (B) **Mantle Cell Lymphoma:** This is an aggressive B-cell lymphoma. While it can present with lymphadenopathy and sometimes involve the blood, the immunophenotype is distinct: typically CD5+, but usually CD23- and positive for cyclin D1 (due to t(11;14) translocation). The morphology is often slightly larger cells than CLL/SLL.
- (C) **Hairy Cell Leukemia:** This is a rare, aggressive B-cell leukemia characterized by pancytopenia, splenomegaly, and distinctive "hairy" cells on peripheral smear (irregular cytoplasmic projections). The immunophenotype is CD19+, CD20+, CD11c+, CD25+, CD103+, and typically negative for CD5 and CD23. This patient's presentation and immunophenotype are inconsistent with HCL.
- (D) **Small Lymphocytic Lymphoma (SLL):** SLL is the tissue-based counterpart of CLL. It is defined by lymphadenopathy and/or splenomegaly with *minimal* peripheral blood involvement (<5,000/µL clonal lymphocytes). This patient has significant peripheral blood involvement (25,000/µL), meeting the criteria for leukemia. Therefore, while the underlying disease process is the same, the clinical presentation with significant leukocytosis points towards CLL.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | CLL                                     | SLL                                     | Follicular Lymphoma                     | Mantle Cell Lymphoma                   | Hairy Cell Leukemia                     |
|----------------------|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|
| **Primary Site**     | Blood/Bone Marrow (≥5,000/µL lymphocytes) | Lymph Nodes/Spleen (<5,000/µL lymphocytes) | Lymph Nodes                             | Lymph Nodes                             | Blood/Bone Marrow/Spleen                |
| **WBC Count**        | Typically elevated (>5,000/µL)           | Typically normal or mildly elevated      | Typically normal                         | Variable                                 | Typically low (pancytopenia)            |
| **Morphology**       | Small, mature lymphocytes                | Small, mature lymphocytes                | Follicular pattern, centroblasts/centrocytes | Small to medium lymphocytes             | "Hairy" cells                           |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+           | CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+           | CD10+, BCL2+, BCL6+                       | CD5+, CD23-, Cyclin D1+                  | CD19+, CD20+, CD11c+, CD25+, CD103+     |
| **Cytogenetics**     | del(13q), del(11q), del(17p), trisomy 12 | del(13q), del(11q), del(17p), trisomy 12 | t(14;18) common                         | t(11;14) common                         | -                                        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
CLL and SLL represent the same underlying B-cell malignancy, distinguished primarily by the site of predominant involvement. CLL involves the blood (≥5,000/µL clonal lymphocytes), while SLL involves lymph nodes/spleen with minimal blood involvement (<5,000/µL). The characteristic immunophenotype (CD5+, CD19+, CD20(dim)+, CD23+) is key for diagnosis. Differentiating from other indolent lymphomas like follicular lymphoma (CD10+) and aggressive lymphomas like mantle cell lymphoma (CD23-, Cyclin D1+) relies on immunophenotyping and clinical context.

---

<a id='question-094'></a>

### Question 094: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-44 (Patients who present with lymph node findings are )
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the molecular pathogenesis of Burkitt lymphoma, specifically the role of the MYC oncogene translocation and its downstream effects on cell proliferation and apoptosis. (考察伯基特淋巴瘤的分子发病机制，特别是MYC癌基因易位及其对细胞增殖和凋亡的下游影响。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents to the clinic with a rapidly enlarging mass on his jaw, causing facial asymmetry and difficulty chewing. He reports a 2-week history of fever, night sweats, and unintentional weight loss. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender mass measuring approximately 10 cm in diameter on the right mandible, along with generalized lymphadenopathy, particularly in the cervical and axillary regions. Laboratory studies show elevated serum lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) levels (1500 U/L, normal <250 U/L) and hyperuricemia (10 mg/dL, normal <7 mg/dL). A biopsy of the jaw mass reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, finely clumped chromatin, multiple small nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are positive for CD20 and CD10, and negative for CD5 and BCL2. Cytogenetic analysis shows a translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus on chromosome 14. Which of the following molecular events is most likely responsible for the pathogenesis of this patient's condition?

(A) Amplification of the BCL2 gene leading to inhibition of apoptosis.
(B) Translocation involving the RUNX1 gene, resulting in the production of fusion proteins that disrupt normal hematopoiesis.
(C) Translocation involving the MYC gene, leading to constitutive overexpression of a transcription factor.
(D) Mutation in the BRAF gene, resulting in constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway.
(E) Translocation involving the IGH gene and the BCL6 gene, leading to dysregulation of B-cell development.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 25-year-old male from Equatorial Africa. This demographic is highly suggestive of endemic Burkitt lymphoma, which is strongly associated with Epstein-Barr virus (EBV) infection in this region.
- **Clinical Presentation:** Rapidly enlarging jaw mass (common site for endemic Burkitt), fever, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), generalized lymphadenopathy. These symptoms point towards a rapidly proliferating lymphoma.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Elevated LDH and hyperuricemia are characteristic of high tumor burden and rapid cell turnover, frequently seen in Burkitt lymphoma due to extensive cell death.
- **Histopathology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with specific nuclear features (round nuclei, clumped chromatin, small nucleoli, scant cytoplasm) is classic for Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunophenotype:** CD20+ and CD10+ are typical markers for B-cell lymphomas, including Burkitt lymphoma. CD5- helps exclude mantle cell lymphoma, and BCL2- helps exclude follicular lymphoma.
- **Cytogenetics:** Translocation involving the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (IGH) on chromosome 14 is a hallmark of Burkitt lymphoma. The most common translocation is t(8;14)(q24;q32), involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the IGH locus.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is a highly aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by a specific chromosomal translocation. The most common translocation, t(8;14)(q24;q32), places the MYC proto-oncogene (located at 8q24) under the control of the highly active immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) enhancer (located at 14q32).
- **MYC Oncogene:** MYC is a transcription factor that regulates genes involved in cell growth, proliferation, differentiation, and apoptosis.
- **Translocation Mechanism:** The translocation leads to constitutive overexpression of the MYC protein. This overexpression drives uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibits apoptosis, leading to the rapid tumor growth characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Other Translocations:** Less common translocations involve MYC and the immunoglobulin light chain loci, t(2;8)(p12;q24) or t(8;22)(q24;q11).
- **EBV Association:** In endemic Burkitt lymphoma, EBV infection is strongly associated. EBV infection can contribute to the pathogenesis by immortalizing B cells and potentially increasing the likelihood of MYC translocations.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Amplification of the BCL2 gene leading to inhibition of apoptosis. This mechanism is characteristic of Follicular Lymphoma, often associated with the t(14;18) translocation, which places BCL2 under the control of the IGH enhancer. While BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein, Burkitt lymphoma is typically BCL2 negative.
- (B): Translocation involving the RUNX1 gene, resulting in the production of fusion proteins that disrupt normal hematopoiesis. RUNX1 gene rearrangements are associated with Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML), particularly AML with inv(16) or t(16;16). This is not characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma.
- (D): Mutation in the BRAF gene, resulting in constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway. BRAF mutations (e.g., V600E) are commonly found in melanoma, papillary thyroid carcinoma, and some colorectal cancers. They are not a primary driver mutation in Burkitt lymphoma.
- (E): Translocation involving the IGH gene and the BCL6 gene, leading to dysregulation of B-cell development. The t(11;14)(q13;q32) translocation involving BCL6 and IGH is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma, leading to overexpression of BCL6. This is not the mechanism for Burkitt lymphoma.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma                                  | Follicular Lymphoma                             | Mantle Cell Lymphoma                            | AML with inv(16)                               |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------|
| **Key Translocation** | t(8;14)(MYC/IGH), t(2;8)(MYC/kappa), t(8;22)(MYC/lambda) | t(14;18)(IGH/BCL2)                             | t(11;14)(IGH/BCL6)                             | inv(16)(p13.1q22) or t(16;16)(p13.1;q22) (RUNX1/RUNX1T1) |
| **Primary Gene**     | MYC                                               | BCL2                                            | BCL6                                            | RUNX1                                           |
| **Cell Type**        | B-cell                                             | B-cell (Follicular center)                       | B-cell (Mantle zone)                             | Myeloid progenitor                              |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2-                               | CD20+, CD10+, BCL2+                              | CD20+, CD5+, BCL2+                              | Myeloid markers (e.g., CD13, CD33)              |
| **Clinical**         | Rapidly growing mass (jaw common), high LDH, hyperuricemia | Indolent, generalized lymphadenopathy, bone marrow involvement | Generalized lymphadenopathy, bone marrow involvement | Fatigue, infection, bleeding, cytopenias        |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is characterized by MYC translocations (most commonly t(8;14)) leading to MYC overexpression and rapid proliferation. Differentiate it from Follicular Lymphoma (t(14;18), BCL2 overexpression), Mantle Cell Lymphoma (t(11;14), BCL6 overexpression), and other leukemias/lymphomas based on cytogenetics, immunophenotype, and clinical presentation. Remember the classic association of endemic Burkitt lymphoma with EBV and jaw involvement in equatorial Africa.

<a id='question-095'></a>

### Question 095: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-45
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular pathogenesis of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML), specifically the role of the BCR-ABL fusion protein as a constitutively active tyrosine kinase and its inhibition by tyrosine kinase inhibitors (TKIs). (考察慢性髓性白血病 (CML) 的分子发病机制，特别是 BCR-ABL 融合蛋白作为持续活性的酪氨酸激酶的作用及其被酪氨酸激酶抑制剂 (TKIs) 抑制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of fatigue, night sweats, and early satiety over the past 3 months. His past medical history is significant for hypertension, managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals mild splenomegaly. A complete blood count (CBC) shows a markedly elevated white blood cell (WBC) count of 180,000/µL with a left shift, including numerous myelocytes, metamyelocytes, and bands. Hemoglobin is 10 g/dL, and platelet count is 600,000/µL. A bone marrow aspirate and biopsy are performed. Cytogenetic analysis of the bone marrow cells reveals the Philadelphia chromosome (Ph chromosome), t(9;22)(q34;q11). Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) confirms the presence of the BCR-ABL fusion gene. The patient is started on imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor. Which of the following molecular targets is directly inhibited by imatinib in this patient?

(A) JAK2
(B) FLT3
(C) BCR-ABL
(D) KIT
(E) ALK

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 58-year-old male with fatigue, night sweats, early satiety (constitutional symptoms), and splenomegaly. These symptoms are common in myeloproliferative neoplasms.
- **CBC Findings:** Markedly elevated WBC count (180,000/µL) with a left shift (immature granulocytes like myelocytes, metamyelocytes, bands), anemia (Hgb 10 g/dL), and thrombocytosis (platelets 600,000/µL). This pattern is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML).
- **Cytogenetics/FISH:** Presence of the Philadelphia chromosome (t(9;22)(q34;q11)) and the BCR-ABL fusion gene. This is the hallmark genetic abnormality of CML.
- **Treatment:** Started on imatinib, a tyrosine kinase inhibitor (TKI).
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular target of imatinib in this patient.
- **Connecting Clues:** The clinical presentation, CBC findings, and especially the presence of the Philadelphia chromosome and BCR-ABL fusion gene strongly point to CML. Imatinib is a known TKI used specifically to treat CML. Therefore, the molecular target of imatinib in this context must be the product of the BCR-ABL fusion gene.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **CML Pathogenesis:** CML is characterized by the reciprocal translocation between chromosomes 9 and 22, t(9;22)(q34;q11). This translocation creates the Philadelphia chromosome and results in the fusion of the *BCR* gene on chromosome 22 with the *ABL1* gene on chromosome 9.
- **BCR-ABL Fusion Protein:** The resulting BCR-ABL fusion protein is a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Unlike the normal ABL protein, which requires specific cellular signals for activation, BCR-ABL is always "on," leading to uncontrolled proliferation and survival of myeloid progenitor cells.
- **Mechanism of Imatinib:** Imatinib (Gleevec) is a small-molecule tyrosine kinase inhibitor specifically designed to target the ATP-binding site of the BCR-ABL kinase domain. By binding to this site, imatinib prevents ATP from binding and phosphorylating downstream substrates, thereby inhibiting the kinase activity of the BCR-ABL protein. This inhibition halts the uncontrolled signaling pathway driving CML.
- **Other Options:**
    - JAK2: A tyrosine kinase involved in cytokine signaling (e.g., erythropoietin, thrombopoietin). Mutations in JAK2 (V617F) are common in Polycythemia Vera, Essential Thrombocythemia, and Primary Myelofibrosis.
    - FLT3: A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in hematopoietic cell development. Mutations in FLT3 (ITD or TKD) are common in Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML).
    - KIT: A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in hematopoiesis, melanogenesis, and other processes. Mutations in KIT are associated with Gastrointestinal Stromal Tumors (GISTs), Mastocytosis, and some forms of AML.
    - ALK: A receptor tyrosine kinase involved in neuronal development. Rearrangements involving ALK (e.g., EML4-ALK fusion) are associated with Anaplastic Large Cell Lymphoma (ALCL) and Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer (NSCLC).

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) JAK2: Incorrect. JAK2 mutations are characteristic of other myeloproliferative neoplasms like Polycythemia Vera and Essential Thrombocythemia, not CML. Imatinib does not primarily target JAK2.
- (B) FLT3: Incorrect. FLT3 mutations are common in Acute Myeloid Leukemia (AML), not CML. Imatinib is not a primary treatment for FLT3-mutated AML (although other TKIs like midostaurin or gilteritinib are used).
- (D) KIT: Incorrect. KIT mutations are associated with GISTs, mastocytosis, and some AML subtypes, not CML. Imatinib can inhibit KIT, but it is not the primary target in this CML patient.
- (E) ALK: Incorrect. ALK rearrangements are associated with ALCL and NSCLC, not CML. Imatinib can inhibit ALK, but it is not the primary target in this CML patient.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature          | CML                                  | Polycythemia Vera (PV)                 | AML (FLT3-ITD)                       | GIST                                  | ALCL                                  |
|-------------------|---------------------------------------|----------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|---------------------------------------|
| **Key Genetic Abnormality** | t(9;22), BCR-ABL fusion             | JAK2 V617F mutation                    | FLT3-ITD mutation                     | KIT mutation                          | ALK rearrangement                     |
| **Primary Molecular Target for TKI** | BCR-ABL                               | JAK2 (Ruxolitinib)                     | FLT3 (Midostaurin, Gilteritinib)      | KIT (Imatinib)                        | ALK (Crizotinib, Alectinib)            |
| **Typical Clinical Presentation** | Fatigue, splenomegaly, leukocytosis | Erythrocytosis, pruritus, thrombosis | Fatigue, bleeding, infection, blasts | Abdominal pain, GI bleeding          | Lymphadenopathy, systemic symptoms    |
| **Hematologic Findings** | Leukocytosis, left shift, basophilia | Erythrocytosis, thrombocytosis         | Blasts in blood/marrow, cytopenias    | Variable, often anemia               | Variable, often lymphocytosis         |
| **Primary TKI Treatment** | Imatinib                             | Ruxolitinib                            | Midostaurin, Gilteritinib             | Imatinib                             | Crizotinib, Alectinib                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The hallmark of CML is the Philadelphia chromosome resulting in the BCR-ABL fusion protein, a constitutively active tyrosine kinase. Imatinib is a first-generation TKI specifically designed to inhibit BCR-ABL. Understanding the specific genetic driver and its targeted therapy is crucial. Key differentials for myeloproliferative neoplasms include PV (JAK2), ET (JAK2/CALR/MPL), and MF (JAK2/CALR/MPL). For AML, FLT3 and NPM1 mutations are common targets. For solid tumors, KIT is relevant for GIST, and ALK for NSCLC/ALCL.

<a id='question-096'></a>

### Question 096: Hematopoietic Pathology: Leukemias and Lymphomas: Focus 02-46 (patients also have lymph node involvement.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Differentiation between B-cell lymphomas based on morphology, immunophenotype, and genetic translocations, specifically distinguishing Burkitt lymphoma from other aggressive B-cell lymphomas like Diffuse Large B-cell Lymphoma (DLBCL) and Follicular Lymphoma.

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old male from Equatorial Africa presents with a 3-week history of rapidly enlarging abdominal masses, fever, night sweats, and weight loss. He denies any prior history of malignancy or immunosuppression. Physical examination reveals significant abdominal distension with palpable masses in the right lower quadrant and epigastrium. Lymphadenopathy is noted in the cervical and inguinal regions. Laboratory studies show elevated lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) at 1200 U/L (normal < 250 U/L), hyperuricemia (uric acid 10 mg/dL, normal < 7 mg/dL), and anemia (hemoglobin 9 g/dL, normal 13.5-17.5 g/dL). A biopsy of an abdominal mass reveals a diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells with round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, and scant cytoplasm. The cells are arranged in sheets with interspersed macrophages (tingible body macrophages) that have phagocytosed apoptotic tumor cells, creating a "starry sky" appearance. Immunohistochemistry shows the tumor cells positive for CD20, CD10, BCL6, and MUM1, but negative for BCL2. Fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH) analysis reveals a translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 and the immunoglobulin heavy chain (IGH) locus on chromosome 14, resulting in t(8;14)(q24;q32).

Which of the following molecular alterations is most characteristic of this patient's condition?

(A) t(11;14)(q13;q32) leading to overexpression of Cyclin D1
(B) t(14;18)(q32;q21) leading to overexpression of BCL2
(C) t(8;14)(q24;q32) leading to overexpression of MYC
(D) t(14;22)(q32;q11) leading to fusion of BCR and ABL1
(E) Point mutation in the BRAF gene leading to constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** Young adult male from Equatorial Africa (endemic region for Burkitt lymphoma).
- **Symptoms:** Rapidly enlarging abdominal masses, fever, night sweats, weight loss (B symptoms), suggesting an aggressive lymphoma.
- **Physical Exam:** Abdominal distension, palpable masses, lymphadenopathy.
- **Labs:** Elevated LDH (high tumor burden/turnover), hyperuricemia (tumor lysis), anemia (bone marrow involvement or chronic disease).
- **Histology:** Diffuse proliferation of medium-sized lymphoid cells, round nuclei, prominent nucleoli, scant cytoplasm, "starry sky" appearance (due to tingible body macrophages engulfing apoptotic cells). This morphology is highly suggestive of Burkitt lymphoma.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD20+ (B-cell marker), CD10+ (germinal center marker), BCL6+ (germinal center marker), MUM1+ (activated B-cell marker). This immunophenotype is characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma. Importantly, BCL2 is negative, which helps distinguish it from Follicular Lymphoma.
- **FISH:** t(8;14)(q24;q32) - This is the hallmark genetic translocation of Burkitt lymphoma, placing the MYC oncogene under the control of the immunoglobulin heavy chain enhancer, leading to MYC overexpression.

The combination of clinical presentation (rapidly growing masses, B symptoms), histology ("starry sky"), immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-), and the specific t(8;14) translocation definitively points to Burkitt lymphoma. The question asks for the characteristic molecular alteration.

##### 2. Pathophysiological, Molecular & Pharmacological Deep-Dive (病理生理、分子机制与药理靶点深度剖析)
- **Burkitt Lymphoma Pathogenesis:** Burkitt lymphoma is an aggressive B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma characterized by a high proliferation rate. The defining genetic event is a chromosomal translocation involving the MYC gene on chromosome 8 (specifically locus 8q24) and one of the immunoglobulin gene loci (most commonly IGH on chromosome 14q32, less commonly IGK on chromosome 2p12 or IGL on chromosome 22q11).
- **Molecular Mechanism:** The translocation places the MYC proto-oncogene (a transcription factor regulating cell growth, proliferation, and apoptosis) under the control of strong immunoglobulin gene enhancers. This leads to constitutive and markedly increased expression of the MYC protein.
- **MYC Overexpression:** Elevated MYC drives uncontrolled cell proliferation and inhibits differentiation, leading to the rapid growth and high mitotic rate characteristic of Burkitt lymphoma. The high cell turnover also explains the elevated LDH and risk of tumor lysis syndrome (leading to hyperuricemia).
- **Immunophenotype:** The tumor cells typically express markers associated with germinal center B-cells (CD10, BCL6) and activated B-cells (MUM1). They are negative for BCL2, distinguishing them from Follicular Lymphoma, which also involves germinal center B-cells but typically has t(14;18) and BCL2 overexpression.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): t(11;14)(q13;q32) leading to overexpression of Cyclin D1. This translocation is characteristic of Mantle Cell Lymphoma. Mantle cell lymphoma typically involves older adults, presents with lymphadenopathy and splenomegaly, and the cells are CD5+, CD19+, CD20+, CD23-. Cyclin D1 overexpression drives cell cycle progression. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma.
- (B): t(14;18)(q32;q21) leading to overexpression of BCL2. This translocation is the hallmark of Follicular Lymphoma. It involves the immunoglobulin heavy chain locus (IGH) on chromosome 14 and the BCL2 gene on chromosome 18. BCL2 is an anti-apoptotic protein, and its overexpression inhibits programmed cell death, contributing to the indolent nature of Follicular Lymphoma. Burkitt lymphoma cells are typically BCL2 negative. This is incorrect.
- (D): t(14;22)(q32;q11) leading to fusion of BCR and ABL1. This translocation, known as the Philadelphia chromosome, is characteristic of Chronic Myeloid Leukemia (CML) and a subset of Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia (ALL). The resulting BCR-ABL1 fusion protein is a constitutively active tyrosine kinase that drives uncontrolled myeloid proliferation. This is incorrect for Burkitt lymphoma, which is a B-cell lymphoma.
- (E): Point mutation in the BRAF gene leading to constitutive activation of the MAPK pathway. BRAF mutations (most commonly V600E) are found in various cancers, including melanoma, colorectal cancer, and hairy cell leukemia. While mutations in the MAPK pathway can occur in some lymphomas, they are not the defining molecular alteration of Burkitt lymphoma. The characteristic genetic lesion in Burkitt lymphoma is the MYC translocation. This is incorrect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点横向对比与鉴别矩阵):
| Feature             | Burkitt Lymphoma              | Follicular Lymphoma          | Mantle Cell Lymphoma         | CML/ALL (Ph+)               |
|----------------------|-------------------------------|------------------------------|------------------------------|------------------------------|
| **Typical Age**      | Children/Young Adults         | Middle-Aged/Elderly          | Middle-Aged/Elderly          | Adults                      |
| **Clinical Course**  | Very Aggressive, Rapid Growth | Indolent, Slow Growth        | Aggressive/Indolent          | Chronic/Acute                |
| **Morphology**       | "Starry Sky"                  | Nodular/Diffuse, Centrocytes | Small lymphocytes, Mantle Zone| Myeloid precursors          |
| **Immunophenotype**  | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2- | CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, BCL2+   | CD5+, CD19+, CD20+, CD23-    | CD34+, CD117+, BCR-ABL1+     |
| **Characteristic Translocation** | t(8;14)(MYC/IGH)            | t(14;18)(IGH/BCL2)           | t(11;14)(CCND1/IGH)          | t(9;22)(BCR/ABL1)            |
| **Key Molecular Driver** | MYC overexpression           | BCL2 overexpression          | Cyclin D1 overexpression     | BCR-ABL1 fusion protein     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Burkitt lymphoma is an aggressive B-cell lymphoma characterized by the t(8;14) translocation leading to MYC overexpression. Key features include rapid growth, "starry sky" histology, and a specific immunophenotype (CD20+, CD10+, BCL6+, MUM1+, BCL2-). Differentiating it from other aggressive lymphomas like DLBCL and indolent lymphomas like Follicular Lymphoma relies on recognizing these distinct features, especially the characteristic translocation and immunophenotype. Remember the key translocations for common lymphomas: t(8;14) for Burkitt, t(14;18) for Follicular, t(11;14) for Mantle Cell, and t(9;22) for CML/Ph+ ALL.
